## 1 — Chapter Header Block

**Name-Chain:**
- Phase 1 — The Roots of Becoming (locked, Part 30 Locked Canon)
- Section 1 — Azure Sky and Gale (locked, Part 30 Locked Canon)
- Novel-Part 1 — The Two Mornings
- Chapter 005 — The House With No Walls

**Chapter Purpose (Part 30 Section-mandate citations, by function name only):**
This Chapter shows the second raid of the Novel-Part, in the story's present, and then Gale on its own ground. It serves the Rival-Kingdom Raids and Skirmishes function (the early part: one early raid on a Gale mine caravan on Phase Day 9, by the Redhaft Vanguard of Brackenhold, seen from the escort captain's side and then from the side of the Vanguard's raid leader, with its report reaching the Gale court on Phase Day 14; the Section's own raids still begin in Novel-Part 3), the World Introduction function (Gale on its own ground: Highgale, the Gale capital on the Aerie Plateau floor, and its eight districts, the Aerie Palace above them, the Court held in the open and the Stone Ring), the Ensemble Introduction function (Aeries and Aalia, each in their own viewpoint and each already wanting something, with the dragon-human heritage of the king read in how his people see him), the First Cultivation Scenes function (Aeries and Aalia each at practice, one named technique each, shown once and never explained; the weapon moves of the escort and of the raiders at the Warrior level), The Alliance Established function by traces (a letter from Aegon, a letter from Varys and two answers) and the technology thread (Gale's end of the *Hourline* planted by absence: the letters come by hand where the wire stops). It keeps setups row 11 (planted again), row 13 (the hidden edge, held back) and The Lone Banner (carried: Gale now holds two reports, one question and one clue it cannot test). It leans on Part 30 Pillar 1 (Roots), Pillar 3 (Loyalty) and Pillar 4 (The World is Larger Than You Know).

**Calendar Window (inside the Novel-Part window; dates plus Phase Day, Part 41 Area 4):**
Year 1247, Ilovany, Day 9 (Phase Day 9), weekday Nuvil, to Year 1247, Ilovany, Day 14 (Phase Day 14), weekday Mavil: six days counted whole, with three dates used, nested inside the Section and Novel-Part windows. Areas 01 and 02 fall on Day 9 (Phase Day 9), weekday Nuvil, from first light, as one scene. Areas 03 and 04 fall on Day 12 (Phase Day 12), weekday Alovil, from the late afternoon to dark, as one scene. Areas 05 and 06 fall on Day 14 (Phase Day 14), weekday Mavil, from the morning into the afternoon, as one scene. The story clock stands on Phase Day 8 after Chapter 004, so every date here falls after it and no step back is planned. Day 1 of the Year is Orvil (the calendar ledger), so Day 9 is Nuvil, Day 12 is Alovil and Day 14 is Mavil (Part 41 Area 3). The days between the raid and the news have reasons in the plan, set in Area 05: the Kite Line does not reach the mine road, the Windward Trail opened to horses only a few days before the raid, the Ridgewatch patrol finds the caravan on its first ride up the road since the Trail opened (Phase Day 11), and the weather stops the Kite Line on the plateau, so the patrol's lieutenant rides to the palace in person and reaches it on Phase Day 14. Varya and Aegon are eight throughout; their birthdays fall after this Chapter. Every line of all three dates reads as the present.

**Threads Carried In:**
- The Alliance: warm and old; Gale is not told of the raid from before Day One, which Varys said he would tell King Aeries himself, "when I have something to tell him that is true, and not before" (Chapter 003); Valia's note to Aalia, four lines sealed with plain wax on Phase Day 2, carries nothing of the raid.
- Messages Between the Kingdoms: the old wire stops at the corridor; the *Hourline* was introduced in Chapter 003 on Azure Sky's side only, and Gale's end is absent.
- The Lone Banner: the raid from before Day One and its open question, how the raiders knew the road and the hour, held at the Azure Sky court alone (Chapters 003 and 004).
- Row 11: planted in Chapter 003 (one raid before the Phase, the first blood of the peace).
- The Friendship: opened in Chapter 004; "not yet" lies between Varya and Aegon, who is lodged in Azorion as a guest of Varys's house for the school term.
- One Sky; row 13, the hidden edge, held back.

**Threads Carried Out:**
- Row 11: planted again, in the story's present: one early raid on a Gale mine caravan on Phase Day 9, the heavier of the two raids of this Novel-Part, since neither its escort nor its hands live.
- The Lone Banner: carried; the Gale court holds two reports (the raid from before Day One, told by Varys, and its own raid, read from the ground by a patrol, since no one of the escort or the caravan lives) and the same question, how the raiders found out, with one clue in hand: the clamp found on the Azure Sky wire, which Varys's letter tells as one plain confirmed fact and which Gale cannot test, because Gale's own end of the wire is absent; no source, no band and no kingdom is named at either court; the reader alone knows whose company struck both; how the raiders found the Gale caravan is not shown to the reader or to Gale; the question is left open on purpose for a later Novel-Part.
- The Alliance: the telling and the warmth, not a test and not the first exchange between the courts (that stays in Novel-Part 2): Varys tells King Aeries himself by his own letter, and the two answers are warm and bind nothing.
- Messages Between the Kingdoms: Gale's end by absence; the news reaches the Gale court by hand where the wire stops, twice.
- The Friendship: carried by Aegon's absence and by his letter home.
- Wulfric Rask: the Tier B villain on the page, in his own viewpoint, with the Redhaft Vanguard; his registration is met (the villain file, Layers L02 and L07 and the Tier B interview of 2026-10-18), and his standing kill state, `B39-L25-KILL-02`, means no first-kill drift is engaged. Hadric Stoll, the Tier C side villain of Chapter 003, rides with the company as a squad leader; he is the one other raider the spec names.
- Row 13, the hidden edge, held back: Aeries and Aalia show no more than their level; the fighters of the early raid carry no hidden edge.
- New places and people of Gale, first use: Highgale and its eight districts, the Stone Ring, the mine Whistledeep and its road, the Ridgewatch detachment, Sigrun Korth's escort, Dagmar Sund.

**Opening hook:** A road at the foot of the Windward Range on the ninth day of spring, a mine's caravan with an escort of six, and a captain who is not easy about the road.
**Closing beat:** Two answers are written in two rooms of the Aerie Palace, the king's to Varys and the queen's to Valia, and the question of how the raiders found out stands unanswered.
**New named things met by the reader this Chapter** (completed from the written Chapter at the Gate 2 of 2026-10-19): in Area 01, the mine Whistledeep (named by its ore), Stirrup Ford, the Seam Road, Shalegate Pass, the Mule Stair, the Ridgewatch outpost at the foot of the stair and the Windward Trail (as "the Trail" in Area 01 and by its full name in Area 05), and the persons Sigrun Korth, Torvek Gant, Yrsa Wold, Jorund Ulm, Ingrith Mott and Egil Hurn; in Area 02, no new name beyond the Named Moves, with Rask and Hadric Stoll returning from Chapter 003; in Area 03, Highgale with its districts Frame Row, Rack Market, Courier Green, Muster Field, Leeside, Weighhouse Row, Elders' Rise and the Ropewalk, the *Kite Line* (a named item, of the type system or service), the ward-line, the Landing Stone, the Wind Crossing, the Stone Vigil and the Stone Ring, and the persons Ranulf Skeld, Ottar Haug, Hallgerd Vorn and Ivar Lund, with Knut Dahl and Hallgerd Vorn's grandson Torkel named in the telling; in Area 04, the stations of the ward-line, the mark called Elbow and the person Ragna Holm; in Area 05, the Ridgewatch detachment and the persons Dagmar Sund, Sverre Vik, Gudrun Nyland and Leif Bakke, with Marta Kell and Ewan Mabry of the corridor post named in speech; Area 06 adds no new name. The Named Moves of the escort, all first used here and taken from the Manual Ledger by exact name: six Basic saber guard moves (***Hooding Backedge***, ***Tight Basket***, ***Ready Backedge***, ***Swell Cowl***, ***Resting Belly*** and ***Yelmancowl***) and moves of the two Intermediate saber manuals (***Basketscreen***, ***Swipe Swat***, ***Swellbarrier***, ***Falsedge Barrier***, ***Yelmanprop***, ***Whirlflog*** and ***Rushing Gallop***); the Named Moves of the raiders first used here (***Keen Axeblade***, ***Cinderheave***, ***Stalling Haft*** and ***Stalwart Socketring***), with ***Brawnhaft***, ***Staticsnatch***, ***Rushing Axeblade***, ***Levering Hookpull***, ***Rubble Axefall***, ***Subjugating Hookback*** and ***Axefall Decree*** returning from Chapter 003; and the two kit techniques ***Vigorrondel*** (Aeries, Area 03) and ***Nickvoyage*** (Aalia, Area 04). Returning by name: the *First Grip*, Brea and Kerra (in Aegon's letter, from Chapters 003 and 004), and, in Varys's letter, the Bluecleft mine, the lower ford and the persons Ansel Roke, Davin Tarn, Wil Harker, Edric Gower and Perrin Lisle (from Chapter 003). The forms of address used for Gale people are "Madam" (Area 04) and "Your Majesty" in a public setting (Areas 03, 05 and 06), with the bare rank nouns "Captain" and "Lieutenant"; "Sire" is not spoken in this Chapter. The substance Power Crystal is an existing substance of the energy law and is no new name. Information only; nothing is counted.
**Build mode:** whole
**Viewpoint:** one viewpoint for each Area block: Sigrun Korth (Area 01), Wulfric Rask (Area 02), Aeries (Area 03), Aalia (Area 04), Aeries (Area 05) and Aalia (Area 06). No Area block is a Dual-POV Area: every change of viewpoint falls between two Area blocks, and a visible break shows at each (Part 38 Rules 2.4.7 and 2.4.12).
**Chapter pacing:** Medium (the Areas run Fast, Fast, Slow, Slow, Medium, Medium: the raid is quick, the day in Gale is wide and slow, and the news and the answers are balanced)
**Chapter size target (words):** 18,000 (the sum of the six Area size targets, 3,000 each; the author's rulings of 2026-10-18: each Area draft is written at no more than 72 percent of its target, about 2,160 words, so the finished Chapter lands at or a little under 12,960 words; 72 percent is the cap and not the aim, and an Area over its cap blocks at Gate 1, so the writer aims a little under 2,160 words for each Area. A scene is paced by cutting other material, never by clipping speech or using a contraction)

## 3 — Standing Exclusions (verify at build, audit, and every amendment)

- **P30-L8 Forbidden Entities (Locked Canon, Authority Tier 0):** the Three Moon Sects (Full Moon Sect, Fallen Moon Sect, Dark Moon Sect), Dragon Mountain, and the Empire of Gods may never be planned into any Phase 1 spec item: no beat, debut, thread, allusion, or open question may involve them. No Area names, shows or hints at the ground north of the Windward Range, and no word is used of anything that lies there; the raiders' track goes up the draw and is lost on the shale, and it is never followed.
- **P30-L9 Sentient Monsters (Locked Canon, Authority Tier 0):** no sentient monster (a talking beast; Part 40 Area 11) may be planned into any Phase 1 spec item: no beat, debut, thread, allusion, rumour, legend, idiom or open question may involve one. No monster is planned in any Area of this Chapter. The mules of the caravan are ordinary animals. Low-grade ore gives off little pressure and draws no beast, so none is shown.
- **Author-removed concepts:** the "Sapphire Line" (ambient hum/frequency/vibration bedrock relay) may never be planned in under any name. The plan uses no hum, frequency, vibration, resonance or bedrock wording for any wire, relay, ward, mine or road; the sound of the Aerie Palace is written as a whistle or a note of the wind. [Extend this list as further removal directives issue.]
- **Author-held card (Brackenhold):** the author holds one fact about Brackenhold's rule that no agent may invent, name, hint at or foreshadow in any plan item, text or note of this Chapter. Rask's passage in Area 02 is the place a hint would be tempting: the page shows his count, his sign, his stroke and his leaving, and gives no reason of the king's beyond what Chapter 003 gave. The information behind the ambush (Engagement Ledgers of Areas 01 and 02) is named only as a kind of source, a leak or a scout's report of the route and the hour, with which of the two left to Novel-Part 3, and it touches nothing the author holds back. The king is named in the narration of Area 02 as Chapter 003 named him and is never shown. Rask's stance toward the king's word is the Core Belief and the Surface Belief of his file (`wulfric-rask` Section 4, read at Chapter Spec 005) and nothing more: no meeting, voice, order, doubt, memory or question of the king's is shown or invented. Gate 1 flags any hint.
- **Wulfric Rask's holds (`wulfric-rask` Sections 1, 4 and 11, read at Chapter Spec 005):** no address form, rank word or honorific of Brackenhold is coined for him or in his mouth (his page words are "Rask" in the narration of his own viewpoint and "the raid leader" as a role); nothing interior about the king is shown or invented; no parley or negotiation with terms, and he says nothing to the captain; contractions and the em dash are barred in his speech and in his thought alike; he has no epigram. A drafting agent who needs more stops and says so, and the author rules. Gate 1 flags any breach.
- **Hadric Stoll's holds (the Tier C row and the behavioral line of `ledger-character-villain/_index.md`, read at Chapter Spec 005):** the page gives his Level as "the sixth" and no stage, because the stage is the author's to set; no Brackenhold address form is coined for him or in his mouth, and he addresses no one by a vocative; his registered conduct is exact work under pressure (the Signature `B39-L03-CHANNEL-03`), the hand resting on the haft, the same checks made in the same order, sight that leads and notices motion, and a mind that gives an order again in ordered steps; nothing is shown or said of what he carries at his belt or of any informant, which stays with the question left open. A drafting agent who needs more stops and says so, and the author rules. Gate 1 flags any breach.
- **Reader-only knowledge (Brackenhold) and what Gale knows:** the reader knows whose company struck the caravan, as Chapter 003 showed it; no Gale person in this Chapter says, hears or suspects whose men they were, and no Gale person names the leader: Rask is named only by the narration of his own viewpoint (Area 02) and is "a weight", "the man at the trees" or nothing at all to the escort. The Gale court knows the raiders as unknown men in plain clothes, and it knows no more of them than the patrol reads on the ground (plain coats, long hafts, a company of about two dozen, a mule-track and their own dead carried out), since no one of the escort or the caravan lives to tell it; no source, no band and no kingdom is named by any Gale person. Neither court learns in this Novel-Part that the raiders were Brackenhold's. The Redhaft Vanguard's name is the narration's word only, as Chapter 003 used it; no character says it.
- **What this Chapter lifts, and only this (carried from the exclusions of Chapters 003 and 004):** the standing "nothing to Gale" is lifted for three items and no others: (a) Varys's handwritten letter, which carries confirmed facts only, among them the wire clamp of Chapter 003 as one plain line (the author's ruling of 2026-10-18), and no guess, no source named, no kingdom named and no new event; (b) Aegon's letter home, written on the evening of Phase Day 8, which says nothing of the ward, the road or the hurt of Phase Day 2 (the earlier bar on those subjects is kept for the content), and nothing of his heritage; (c) the two answers, which bind nothing. Anything else sent to Gale stays excluded. Valia's note of Phase Day 2 is not shown.
- **Row 13, the hidden edge (carried):** Aeries and Aalia show no more than their level to the people of the story; no dragon form, no part-form and no form specifics are shown, named or hinted, and no scale, wing or flight of either is on the page; the fighters of the early raid carry no hidden edge, which is the eight protagonists' alone; Aegon's heritage is neither named nor hinted in his letter; Aeries's night flights and night office are not planted anywhere in this Chapter.
- **Aalia's rendering constraint (`aalia`, Sections 1 and 11):** her spatial faculty is shown as conduct only. The prose shows the act and never narrates her calculating, measuring or using the sense, never names the faculty as active, and renders no spatial arithmetic: no counted paces, no estimated widths, no numeric intervals in her viewpoint or her mouth. Her interior is imagery and placement, not sentences. Her answers contain the answer and nothing else, and she gives a conclusion with no reasoning behind it. Violation is a Check #20 finding.
- **Aeries's registered habits (`aeries`, Sections 1 and 11):** his speech is long, unhurried and unqualified; he pauses only before a promise, and the pause is the whole of the exception; a fragment of his speech is either inside the plain humor or a mistake; weather and pressure words surface in his speech only when something has alarmed him; he paraphrases on purpose, and an exact quotation of another person's words is a marked act with a reason; he will not act on a written account until a voice stands behind it; he never interrupts and never permits an interruption; he coins no maxim; his interior voice is plural by registration; a sincere wind-invocation is interior and is never spoken. Nothing sworn under time pressure, and a promise to anyone but Aalia is never fast.
- **No promise, no war, no exchange:** no commitment is made by either court in this Chapter: no kingdom declares war, no muster is called, no joint action is scheduled and no formal exchange between the courts takes place (the first exchange is in Novel-Part 2, and the Alliance's first test begins in Novel-Part 3). Gale binds nothing in writing: the two answers are the warmth of two houses and bind nothing.
- **Dropped from this Chapter by the author's ruling of 2026-10-18:** the small ordinary promise made at the open ring, courier-flights as a scene (a pilot may land and speak; the setting of the day is not the courier-flights), Aeries's night office and Aeries's night flight. None is planned in any Area, and none may be moved into this Chapter by a drafting agent.
- **Child-harm limit (Part 28 Rule 12.3.2):** no wound, injury or death inflicted on a child under twelve (Part 16 band YC-1) is shown. Aegon appears only in his letter, in a child's voice and in his father's and mother's thought. No child is on the page in this Chapter; the carters and the escort are adults.
- **Latent moves (Part 42 Rule 42.13.1.3; `eligibility-rules.md` E3):** a move that a person's realm cannot execute is known and never shown executed. Rask's Advanced and Elite moves of `MAN-SIG-HAL-013` (`MV-SIG-HAL-013-07`, `-08` and `-09`) are known and never shown. The Intermediate-type moves of `MAN-INT-SAB-003` and `MAN-INT-SAB-007` are shown by the escort's Tier D people only if the earlier Basic saber manual is also shown on the page by its movements in action, because such a person has no kit and the link codes count as met only if the scene has shown the earlier manual (E3); Area 01 therefore shows each of the six with one Basic saber guard move, before or beside the moves of the Intermediate manuals. The page shows a manual by its movements and never by a book. The kit moves of Aeries and Aalia are taken from their ledger kits by exact name; each is a quiet move within their level.
- **Dialogue standard (Part 29 Rules 29.2.1.1c and 29.2.1.7a, Canon Amendment 2026-10-16):** no contraction in dialogue or in narration, ever; every speaker uses full grammatical sentences that answer and build on the line before, in turns usually of two to four connected sentences (shorter only for a real interruption or shock); no chain of clipped fragments, and no statement-comma-clipped-jab pattern; this holds for orders in a fight, for a report and for banter. Dagmar Sund's report is "an ordinary count" in its content (the numbers, the places, the days) and not in its shape: she gives the numbers inside full, connected sentences, and the report is never a tally, a list or a clipped run for the whole beat. A scene is paced by cutting other material, never by clipping speech. Gate 1 flags any breach.
- **Address of Gale people (the author's ruling of 2026-10-18, which supersedes the ledger text on the points named here):** Gale people say "Sire" to the king and "Madam" to the queen in a private setting and "Your Majesty" to either in a public setting. "Wind-Crown", "Storm-Counsel", the Gale spoken acknowledgment and its completions (the name given back, the word "Speak") and wind-names are not used. Keep it simple: Gale people are called by birth name, and a soldier is called by the bare rank noun ("Captain", "Lieutenant") and nothing else, as `gale-kingdom-military` §6.11 already holds. A setting is public when the Court sits in the open or a stranger or the people stand by (Areas 03 and 05 on the open terrace, and Area 06 on the terrace while the lieutenant, the pilot, the steward and the Court's people are present), and private when the king or the queen is with their own household or alone with one person (the ward-line, the palace's inner places, and Area 06 once each goes to their own rooms). Aegon says "Father" and "Mother" in a letter, and "Your Majesty" only in a public setting, which a letter is not. The ledger text that this ruling changes is left to the ledger step at the Gate 2.
- **Cultivation Visibility (Part 29 Rule 29.12.1.3, Canon Amendment 2026-10-16):** every named person who appears has a stated cultivation level and a stated way the reader learns it, set out in section 5.0a; every combatant's level is known to the reader before or at the first blow, and the cross-level moments are named; levels are set in bold-italic where they are written as designations (Rule 29.2.1.1d) and never given as a stat dump. No person on the page holds a sealed level. A person without a level or a route is an open item for the author.
- **Battle Plausibility (Part 44, Canon Amendment 2026-10-17):** Areas 01 and 02 hold the fight, and each carries an Engagement Ledger under its Fight line, made at this build and fixed before drafting; Areas 03 to 06 hold no fight. The gap arithmetic (positions, gaps, bands, anchors) lives in the ledgers and the Fight lines only and never reaches a scene anchor, a beat, narration or dialogue; the page shows level strings, counts, formation, ground and sensed comparison in words (far above, a little above, about equal, no match; Rules 44.5.3.1 to 44.5.3.3). No registered gifted fighter and no limit break is used in the fight. Gate 1 Check #23 reads all of it.
- **Sensing (the author's ruling of 2026-10-17, carried):** a person of the Warrior realm or above senses by soul sense at the ranges of Part 14 Area 4.1 (fifty paces for a Warrior and five hundred for a Ruler, as Chapter 003 printed them); a person of the Senior realm or below senses only by felt pressure at arm's length; a person who is unawakened, and a Mortal Tier 1 person, is read as absence. A company that holds its qi drawn in is not sensed until it lets the qi go. Ground mist hides the eye and does not dull soul sense; the escort's unease is that its sense finds nothing where a company of men would be.
- **Presence (Part 44 Rule 44.7.1.1; Part 3 Areas 17.2 and 17.4):** a Ruler's aura collapses Tier 1 beings in range. Aeries (Ruler Level 2 Mid) and Aalia (Ruler Level 1 Mid) share open ground with petitioners, pilots and guards, so each holds qi drawn in to nothing, as the written Chapters already show for the kings and Valia. No Ruler stands on the field of the raid.
- **Gale's spoken-word culture (carried from `gale-kingdom-state`):** nothing binding is written; a promise is made standing, outdoors, before named witnesses; a paper promise is distrusted, and being asked to put a thing in writing is a small insult. The letters of this Chapter are talk set down and bind nothing, and Aeries's wired notice of Chapter 001 already made the joke that writing it down makes it a joke at home. A Gale person who brings a letter brings a spoken word behind it.
- **The village link (carried from Section Spec 1.1 v1.4):** the Whistledeep road, Stirrup Ford and Shalegate Pass lie well clear of the border village's border; no village word and no village person appears; the reader's first sight of the link between Brackenhold and the village's hostile neighbour is still Chapter 006, from something Bison sees.
- **Power Crystal (the energy law):** the ore is bulk Power Crystal of low grade (a plan reading of Grades 1 to 3), safe for the carters at close range (Grade 5 and above is not, `energy-substance-law` Area 8.4) and drawing no beast. No grade is stated on the page beyond "low". The cargo is called Power Crystal or ore and is never called a shard: a Shard is a later state of the Five-State Taxonomy, and its first appearance follows the Narrative Introduction Sequence Rule of the energy law.
- **Timeline facts of the lag:** the raid is on Phase Day 9 and the news reaches the Gale court on Phase Day 14. The reasons are the Kite Line (it does not reach the mine road, and the weather stops it on the plateau, so the lieutenant rides to the palace in person), the Windward Trail (opened to horses a few days before the raid), and the Ridgewatch patrol (it finds the caravan on its first ride up the road since the Trail opened); the finding is reported and never shown, and what the patrol knows of the raiders it reads from the ground, since no one lives to tell it. Varys's letter and Aegon's letter travel by hand where the wire stops.
- **Names (Part 28 Area 20):** the author's own names are written exactly: Whistledeep, Highgale, the eight district names, the Stone Ring, Sigrun Korth, Torvek Gant, Yrsa Wold, Jorund Ulm, Ingrith Mott, Egil Hurn, Dagmar Sund. The road, ford, pass and stair names come from the author's cleared set. "Stone Ring" shares its second word with the Manual Ledger's cultivation moves (for example ***Endless Ring***), which are moves and not places; no Area uses "Ring" as the name of a move.

## 4 — P30-L5 Crisis-Density Tracking

**This chapter carries:** combat (the early raid of Phase Day 9, Areas 01 and 02: a Gale mine escort destroyed by a company of the Redhaft Vanguard) and a political crisis in small (the telling by letter, the second report and the question left open, Areas 05 and 06); no cultivation breakthrough; no major revelation to any character (the reader alone sees whose company it was)
**Rolling 10-chapter window status:** four earlier Chapters (001 to 004), of which Chapter 003 (the back-dated raid) carries a qualifying element and Chapter 004 carries one if its practice duels count as combat; with this Chapter, 3 of the 5 Chapters written or planned so far carry one if the practice duels count as combat (information only; Law P30-L5 names about 6 of any 10 as its guide)

### 5.0 — Scene Map

*One scene for each run of Area blocks, as the plan stands: Areas 01 and 02 are one scene on Day 9, Areas 03 and 04 are one scene on Day 12, and Areas 05 and 06 are one scene on Day 14. The scene numbers continue those of Chapters 001 to 004, which hold S1 to S12, so this Chapter's scenes are S13, S14 and S15. A scene carries the date line of its day only where its day differs from the scene before it; here all three differ, so each scene prints its date line directly after its break. A break shows before each scene and wherever the same scene changes viewpoint; every scene here changes viewpoint once, between its two Area blocks, so one break shows inside each scene (Rule 2.4.12). No scene crosses a Chapter seam.*

### Area 01 — First Light at Stirrup Ford
**Purpose:** The Chapter opens on the early raid of Phase Day 9, in the captain's viewpoint. A Gale mine caravan from Whistledeep, carrying bulk Power Crystal of low grade down the Seam Road to the Aerie Artificers' College, halts on a shale ledge above Stirrup Ford at first light, with ground mist lying on the water and the low road. Its escort is Captain Sigrun Korth of the Windward Host, riding in person, and five warriors; each of the five is met by what he or she does at the halt, while the captain recites her standing orders and places her people by name and Level. She is not easy about the road: her sense finds nothing in the trees across the ford where a company of men would feel like something, and at the edge of the mist a man in a good coat walks away along the ditch with his hands in his sleeves; she gives him one look. The wagons enter the ford. The company lets its qi go and takes the lead guards in the mist; she sounds her horn once and nothing answers; she holds the two squad leaders and cuts down the first line fighters, and the mist splits her escort. The Area ends on a weight far above her walking out of the trees, which her knees feel before her eyes find it.
**Want, obstacle, stake:** Korth wants to bring the caravan across Stirrup Ford and down the Seam Road to the plateau with her people, her hands and the ore whole. The mist, a ford that strings the wagons out, a road her sense reads as wrong and a company that fights as one body stand in her way. The stake is the lives of her five and of the five hands, and the ore and the manifest. Planned turn: the company lets its qi go. Planned outcome: loss, begun.
**Viewpoint:** Sigrun Korth (Tier D viewpoint holder, a Captain of the Windward Host, Warrior Level 7 High)
**Dual-POV Area:** no
**Area status:** WRITTEN(1.1.1.005)

**Plan of the Area**
- **Pacing (planned):** Fast, because the Area is an ambush, a fight and a turn (the Part 2 anchor-type defaults for combat); the halt that opens it is short and cut to what the fight needs
- **Size target:** 3,000 words (a custom figure in a whole Chapter)
- **Budget stretch:** up to 0.72 of target; the draft aims a little under 2,160 words, the cap of 0.72 of target, because the Area carries a short halt, six people to be met by what they do, an ambush in mist, a fight with named moves and a turn in one Fast run, as the Chapter's opener; if the budget presses, the writer drops Intermediate moves and never the Basic guard move behind them, because the links of the Intermediate manuals count as met only while the Basic move has been shown (Part 42 eligibility E3)
- **Scene anchors:** (1) first light and mist: the Day 9 date line stands at the top of the Chapter; the caravan halts on the shale ledge above Stirrup Ford on the Seam Road at the foot of the Windward Range, with ground mist on the water and the low road and sight reaching only a few paces; four wagons of low-grade Power Crystal in sacks, drawn by mules, with the strongbox wagon in the middle of the file; the captain recites her standing orders aloud at the halt (a commander's standing orders are spoken and kept by no copy, `gale-kingdom-military` section 6.8), and in the recital she places her five, each by name and Level in a plain clause; each of the five is met by what he or she does: Torvek Gant palms each wheel hub, Yrsa Wold draws the road in the shale dust before she speaks, Jorund Ulm does not leave the strongbox bench, Ingrith Mott talks to the mules, Egil Hurn laughs, short and early; the four carters and the clerk are met as the people she answers for; the mine is named once, plainly, as Whistledeep, and the ore as bound for the Aerie Artificers' College; (2) the road that is not easy: her soul sense finds nothing in the trees across the ford where a company would feel like something, and no bird calls in the mist; the Windward Trail has only just opened and the road is empty of other travellers; at the edge of the mist a man in a good coat walks away along the ditch with his hands in his sleeves, and she gives him one look and no more (the repeat clue of Chapter 003, never explained); she does not turn the file back, because the standing order is to cross and the ore is due on the plateau; (3) the crossing: the lead wagon enters the ford and the wagons string out across the water, the strongbox wagon in the middle of the stream; (4) the company lets go: its qi arrives all at once, the lower body gives way, and the first rank comes out of the mist on the lead guards, each haft drawn from its sacking in one pull with the wrap let fall behind (***Brawnhaft***), then ***Rushing Axeblade*** and ***Levering Hookpull***; Torvek Gant meets the first rush with ***Tight Basket*** and Egil Hurn with ***Yelmancowl***; the captain sounds her horn once and nothing answers, the mist taking the note; the lead carter at the lead wheel and one more go down; (5) the captain's stand: she steps down onto the gravel bar between the strongbox wagon and the third wagon with the water at her back, and holds the two squad leaders and the line, first with ***Hooding Backedge*** and then with ***Basketscreen*** and ***Swipe Swat***, cutting down the first line fighters, one of whom is thrown into the shallows by the lead wheel and is hauled out of his coat by his own side, and another whose sleeve her cut leaves hanging on the third wagon's tailboard; the mist splits her five from her and from one another, and in it she sees Yrsa Wold hold the strongbox wagon with ***Ready Backedge***, Jorund Ulm on his bench with ***Swell Cowl*** and Ingrith Mott at the mule line with ***Resting Belly***, and she hears the clerk's cry cut off, a third carter's with it, and Egil Hurn's laugh stop; (6) the turn: a weight far above her walks out of the trees on the near bank, and her knees feel it before her eyes find it (the end)
- **Continuation:** the scene (S13) continues in Area 02, which changes the viewpoint to Wulfric Rask at the same moment; where this Area ends the lead guards are down, the escort is split by the mist, the captain holds the gravel bar against the two squad leaders and three line fighters, the horn has sounded once and nothing has answered, and a weight has walked out of the trees, so the tension stays live
- **Planned time skips:** none
- **Fight (optional):** E2, an unofficial skirmish of fewer than fifty fighters (about 30 take part: the company of twenty-four and the escort of six; the five hands do not fight); shape: ambush on an escorted caravan at a ford; sides: the Gale mine escort (a Captain of the Windward Host at Warrior Level 7 High and five warriors at Warrior Levels 3 to 4, with four carters and a clerk who do not fight) against a company of the Redhaft Vanguard of Brackenhold (twenty-four in plain coats with halberds in sacking: Wulfric Rask, two squad leaders (Hadric Stoll at Warrior Level 6 and one at Warrior Level 7 Low) and twenty-one line fighters at Warrior Levels 3 to 5; Rask is not engaged in this Area); terrain: Stirrup Ford, where the Seam Road crosses the stream on a gravel bar at the foot of the Range, with ground mist on the water and the low road; stakes: the lives of the file, the ore and the manifest; cost: the lead guards; Named Moves (the registered names of the Manual Ledger, by ID; a move marked reused was first written in Chapter 003, and a move marked new is first used here): for the company, ***Brawnhaft*** (MV-INT-HAL-011-01, reused), ***Rushing Axeblade*** (MV-INT-HAL-012-03, reused), ***Levering Hookpull*** (MV-INT-HAL-005-10, reused), ***Keen Axeblade*** (MV-INT-HAL-011-02, new), ***Cinderheave*** (MV-INT-HAL-005-09, new) and ***Stalling Haft*** (MV-INT-HAL-011-06, new), and for the squad leaders ***Staticsnatch*** (MV-INT-HAL-004-09, reused, the move of Hadric Stoll) and ***Stalwart Socketring*** (MV-INT-HAL-005-08, new), all from the halberd manuals the Vanguard lends; for the escort, all new, from the Common saber stock the Windward Host lends: one Basic saber guard move for each of the six, shown first so that the links of the Intermediate manuals count as met (Part 42 eligibility E3), the captain's ***Hooding Backedge*** (MV-BAS-SAB-005-01), Torvek Gant's ***Tight Basket*** (MV-BAS-SAB-008-03), Yrsa Wold's ***Ready Backedge*** (MV-BAS-SAB-011-03), Jorund Ulm's ***Swell Cowl*** (MV-BAS-SAB-002-04), Ingrith Mott's ***Resting Belly*** (MV-BAS-SAB-005-03) and Egil Hurn's ***Yelmancowl*** (MV-BAS-SAB-011-05); then the moves of the two Intermediate manuals, the captain's ***Basketscreen*** (MV-INT-SAB-003-05) and ***Swipe Swat*** (MV-INT-SAB-007-05), with ***Swellbarrier*** (MV-INT-SAB-003-03) for Torvek Gant, ***Falsedge Barrier*** (MV-INT-SAB-003-04) for Yrsa Wold, ***Yelmanprop*** (MV-INT-SAB-007-03) for Jorund Ulm, ***Whirlflog*** (MV-INT-SAB-007-01) for Ingrith Mott and ***Rushing Gallop*** (MV-INT-SAB-007-02) for Egil Hurn; no name is coined; offset: ambush on the unready (b): the mist, the first strike, the company's qi held in until the sign, and the rear rank and the pack-line placed before first light; numbers (a): twenty-four against six; turning point: the company lets its qi go; end class: not ended here, the fight runs on into Area 02; shape: an ambush at a ford, which differs from the raid of Chapter 003 (an ambush on a road by a forest edge at dusk) by its ground, its hour, its mist and its end, the escort destroyed to the last person
- **Engagement Ledger (Part 44 Area 5.2; made at the build and fixed before drafting; a plan record that establishes nothing):** One E2 skirmish runs across Areas 01 and 02, so each of the two Areas has its own ledger (Rule 44.5.2.5). The company's strength, ranks and levels below are plan values (the author gave the company as "20 and more raiders", fixed Rask's level, confirmed the ranks' levels and the unnamed squad leader's level as drafted on 2026-10-18 and set Hadric Stoll's Level at 6 with the stage left open; the count of twenty-four and the split into three ranks are plan values). The Gate 2 writes the Vanguard's strength into `redhaft-vanguard` as a first-use fact.
  - **Engagement:** Area 01 (1.1.1.005.01); E2 skirmish; shape: ambush on an escorted caravan; ground: Stirrup Ford, a shallow ford of the stream at the foot of the Range, where the Seam Road crosses on a gravel bar with the road cut into the bank on either side; one feature: the ground mist on the water and the low road, which hides the file and the company from the eye and does not hide them from soul sense (the company holds its qi in until the sign).
  - **Anchors:** Company: Wulfric Rask, Warrior Level 9 Peak (`wulfric-rask` Section 2), not engaged in this Area and felt only as held-in weight in the trees until the last moment; the strongest engaged are the unnamed squad leader at Warrior Level 7 Low and Hadric Stoll at Warrior Level 6, whose stage is not set, so the ledgers read his Level as a range, Level 6 Low to Level 6 Peak. Escort: Sigrun Korth, Warrior Level 7 High (the author's value of 2026-10-18). Anchor gap: Rask over Korth is 9 minor steps, Shut, the captain below (met in Area 02). In this Area the captain is above the unnamed leader by 2 steps and above Stoll by 3 to 6 steps, whichever stage he holds.

Year 1247, Ilovany, Nuvil, Day 9 (Phase Day 9), first light; Kind present. A cold spring dawn, mist on the water and the low road, the Trail only just open. The near bank is the caravan's own side of the stream, the side of the halt ledge, where the trees come down behind the file; the far bank lies toward the plateau; the draw climbs above the ford on the near bank.

### Area 02 — The Count and the Sign
**Purpose:** The same moment from the raid leader's side. Wulfric Rask holds the viewpoint (a Tier B villain by the author's ruling, registered for it: the villain file and Layers L02 and L07 are written and the Tier B interview is held, `wulfric-rask` v2.0). He stood among the trees with his qi drawn in to nothing, counting the file in with his lips shut; his raised and lowered right hand sent the first rank at the lead, and a second lift sent the center. He counts the escort aloud and gives an order in flowing, connected sentences with no reason attached, reads the captain by soul sense, leaves her to his two squad leaders, Hadric Stoll and one other, until the lead is clean and then walks out, the only walking on that road, as he did on the lower road. He ends the captain himself, with his halberd and ***Subjugating Hookback***; ***Axefall Decree*** presses the last three at the strongbox wagon to the ground and costs him, so that he leans on the haft for a count of three. The company takes the ore, on its own pack-mules waiting in the draw above the ford, and the manifest from the strongbox; the hands who stood in the way are killed, and the rear rank goes under the wagons and finds the rest, so that none of the five hands is left alive. The company clears what it can see, since its raid leader holds that nothing is left on the road; this Area shows the clearing and never a trace of what the clearing missed, which shows only in Areas 01 and 05. Rask raises his right hand and keeps it raised, and the company goes up the draw on it with its own fallen, Rask last, the haft for a staff. The reader sees the sign and the count that Chapter 003 showed and knows the company again. The narration names the king as Chapter 003 named him ("Brackenhold's king") and the company by Rask's name alone; no Gale person names him. His stance toward the king's word is carried as a stance only, with no meeting, voice, doubt or memory of the king shown.
**Want, obstacle, stake:** Rask wants the ore and the manifest taken clean and quickly, and the company out of the ford before anything can answer. The captain's stand, the mist that hides his own people from one another and the horn that may yet be answered stand in his way. The stake is the company's clean leaving. Planned turn: his walk into the open. Planned outcome: win, at a small cost to the company.
**Viewpoint:** Wulfric Rask (Tier B villain, Warrior Level 9 Peak)
**Dual-POV Area:** no
**Area status:** WRITTEN(1.1.1.005)

**Plan of the Area**
- **Pacing (planned):** Fast, because the Area is the end of an ambush: a stand, a stroke and a leaving (the Part 2 anchor-type defaults for combat)
- **Size target:** 3,000 words (a custom figure in a whole Chapter)
- **Budget stretch:** up to 0.72 of target; the draft aims a little under 2,160 words, the cap of 0.72 of target, because the Area carries the raid leader's first viewpoint, the captain's end, the cost of the stroke, the take and the leaving, and the Chapter's two repeat clues, the sign and the count, in one Fast run
- **Scene anchors:** (1) the same moment from the trees: it opens live on Rask on the near bank at the trees' edge, standing as the trees stood, his qi drawn in to nothing, reading the gravel bar in the last moments before he goes down to it (the captain a steady weight a little above anything else there, two of the centre rank already at her feet); the count-in and the sign follow straight after as recollection, in two short paragraphs: the count made before the light, with the company in a line at the trees' edge and the long hafts in their sacking stood butt-down in the soft ground (the people, the wagons, the guards, and the company's ranks and Levels), then his right hand rising to the height of his shoulder and coming down once to send the first rank, a second lift to send the center, no word starting the company; the narration names Brackenhold's king as Chapter 003 did, and carries Rask's stance toward the king's word in one plain clause and no more (no meeting, voice, doubt or memory of the king); (2) the count and the order: the one note of the horn goes out and nothing answers, and he counts the silence; he counts the escort aloud and gives an order in flowing, connected sentences with no reason attached, to his squad leaders and the center rank; (3) the captain's stand, seen from his side: she holds the two squad leaders and the line on the gravel bar; Hadric Stoll, the squad leader of the sixth Level, gives his squad the order again in ordered steps and works her flank with ***Staticsnatch***, more exact and more careful as the stand holds (his Signature); Rask reads her by soul sense (high in the seventh Level), sees her cut down the first line fighters, and sends the rear rank across the ford to cut the road behind the file; (4) the walk: when the lead is clean he walks into the open road, the only walking on it; the captain feels his weight and her knees ache before his stroke arrives; he puts the hook behind her knee (***Subjugating Hookback***) and ends her himself, cleanly and quickly, as he has ended others before this story (`B39-L25-KILL-02`, his standing state; no name, place or event of those is shown or invented), and does not look at what he has done; (5) the stroke: the last three of the escort hold the strongbox wagon; ***Axefall Decree*** comes across the gravel bar and presses them to the ground, and the center rank finishes them; he stands with his shoulders heaving and leans on the haft for a count of three; (6) the take: the pack-mules come down from the draw; the sacks go from the wagons to the mules; the manifest is taken from the strongbox; the hands who stood in the way are dead, and the rear rank goes under the wagons and finds the last carter under the third wagon, so that Rask's count of the hands, begun when he counted the file in, closes at five and none left; it is done in the quiet of the line, with no cry and no name heard on the road, and he gives no reason; the killing of the hands is not drawn, and the page stays with his count; (7) the leaving, in two marked beats: (7a) the sign: he raises his right hand and keeps it raised, and the company goes back up the draw on that sign with the ore on the mules, the manifest and its own fallen carried out first; (7b) the last man out: Rask goes last, covering the retreat himself, the haft for a staff; he does not look toward the edge of the mist, and the viewpoint shows no trace of what the clearing missed (the traces show only in Areas 01 and 05)
- **Continuation:** complete scene in this Area, ending the scene S13; where it ends the company has gone up the draw, the escort and the hands are dead, the wagons stand in the ford with the strongbox open, and the question of how the company knew the road and the hour is on the page and not answered
- **Planned time skips:** none
- **Fight (optional):** E2, the end of the skirmish of Area 01; shape: ambush on an escorted caravan, the finish; sides and terrain as in Area 01; stakes: the escort's last stand and the take; cost: the escort wholly; the company's five line fighters; Named Moves (by ID; reused means first written in Chapter 003 and new means first used here): for Rask, ***Subjugating Hookback*** (MV-SIG-HAL-013-04, reused) and ***Axefall Decree*** (MV-SIG-HAL-013-10, reused); for the squad leaders, Hadric Stoll's ***Staticsnatch*** (MV-INT-HAL-004-09, reused) and the other leader's ***Stalwart Socketring*** (MV-INT-HAL-005-08, new) and ***Rubble Axefall*** (MV-INT-HAL-011-05, reused); for the line ***Keen Axeblade*** (MV-INT-HAL-011-02, new), ***Cinderheave*** (MV-INT-HAL-005-09, new), ***Stalling Haft*** (MV-INT-HAL-011-06, new) and ***Rushing Axeblade*** (MV-INT-HAL-012-03, reused); for the escort, all new and shown in Area 01 first with their Basic saber guard moves, the captain's ***Basketscreen*** and ***Swipe Swat***, with ***Falsedge Barrier***, ***Yelmanprop*** and ***Whirlflog***; Rask's Advanced and Elite moves (`MV-SIG-HAL-013-07`, `-08`, `-09`) are known and never shown; offset: numbers (a), ambush and preparation (b), and a small part of exhaustion (d): the captain has cut through the line before he comes, and her breath is spent; no registered gifted fighter and no limit break; turning point: his walk into the open; end class: TT-1 for the escort and TT-4 for the company (the ore and the manifest seized), victory class VC-1 (Total Victory: the escort eliminated, the objective secured); the engagement ends in the destruction of the escort, a loss for Gale as the plan intends
- **Engagement Ledger (Part 44 Area 5.2; made at the build and fixed before drafting; a plan record that establishes nothing):**

  - **Anchors:** Company: Wulfric Rask, Warrior Level 9 Peak (`wulfric-rask` Section 2). Escort: Sigrun Korth, Warrior Level 7 High. Anchor gap: 9 minor steps, Shut, the captain below. Rask is one body and is in one place at a time (Rule 44.2.2.3); the squad leaders (Hadric Stoll at Warrior Level 6, stage not set, read as a range, and one leader at Warrior Level 7 Low) and the line hold the rest of the field while he walks to the bar.

### Area 03 — The Wind Over Highgale
**Purpose:** Gale on its own ground, three days after the raid, with no word of it. The Court has risen on the open terrace of the Aerie Palace, and the Area opens on the king reading the wind from the palace's open edge: a descending sweep through his senses (pressure, heat, movement, breath) that gives the reader scale and context before it narrows to one man. The sweep carries the plateau under the Windward Range, the Kite Line's masts on the high ground, the road down to Highgale and the city's eight districts, each with a one-clause picture on first use, the empty stones of the Stone Ring, and then the man at the open edge. The king goes down to meet the last petitioners as they cross the terrace (the king who comes to you, the unhurried hearing, the remembered life), and the page shows how his people see him: with their voices lowered and without kneeling, and in what they say of the old blood without naming a form; the birds leave the colonnade as he crosses, and a petitioner's dog will not lie down. At the open edge, after the public self, he shows ***Vigorrondel*** once. A pilot of the courier-flights lands at the terrace's edge with a letter from Aegon, written on the evening of Phase Day 8 and held at the corridor post for a flight, where the wire stops. The king reads it standing, takes one full breath, and the grey of his eyes goes deeper for a second; the pilot looks away. The Area ends on a boy's question that his father has to answer truthfully, with the queen's step coming along the ward-line.
**Want, obstacle, stake:** Aeries wants his son to have a childhood in which the sky is not a duty (his Stated Want); on this evening he wants only to put down the public self and read what the wind says. The hearing that ran past its hour, the people who must see him whole, and a letter that asks him the question he has bought every year not to answer stand in the way. The stake is the boy's childhood and the king's own hold on himself. Planned turn: the letter. Planned outcome: a quiet loss, a question taken in.
**Viewpoint:** Aeries (hero, King of Gale, Ruler Level 2 Mid)
**Dual-POV Area:** no
**Area status:** WRITTEN(1.1.1.005)

**Plan of the Area**
- **Pacing (planned):** Slow, because the Area is a world introduction told through a king's senses, a hearing and a quiet practice (the Part 2 anchor-type defaults for world introduction and practice)
- **Size target:** 3,000 words (a custom figure in a whole Chapter)
- **Budget stretch:** up to 0.72 of target; the draft aims a little under 2,160 words, the cap of 0.72 of target, because the Area is the novel's first sight of Gale and of the king: a wide sweep that names a capital and eight districts, a hearing, a named technique and a letter in one Slow run
- **Scene anchors:** (1) the sweep: the Day 12 date line follows the break that opens the scene; a descending sweep through the king's senses at the turn of the day, from the steady easterly under the Windward Range to the terraced plateau, the Kite Line's masts with their chains whistling on the high ground, the road down to Highgale about twelve kilometres to the east-south-east, and the city's eight districts met in use, each by one thing read in it at the day's end and one clause of picture on first use (Frame Row, where the storm-kite frames are bound and mended; Rack Market, where herbs dry on racks in the wind; Courier Green, where the glider pilots land and rest; Muster Field, where the Windward Host and the Wind-Sworn Militia drill; Leeside, the sheltered streets on the western side out of the easterly; Weighhouse Row, where ore and grain are weighed and sealed; Elders' Rise, where the wind-academy monasteries stand; Ropewalk, the long strip where cord and kite-line are laid and twisted), then the palace that has no walls, the wards at its edge, the Stone Ring standing empty with the Court risen, and the man at the open edge, already wanting something; the descent is three paragraphs and not a tour; (2) the last petitioners: two petitioners whose small business was not reached before the Court rose (Tier D by role); the steward and the petitioners say "Your Majesty" (the terrace is open and others stand by); the beat is taken in three marked parts: (2a) the king crosses to them rather than calling them; (2b) he hears them out in full, and the hearing runs past its hour and costs him the evening's margin; (2c) he asks after a person of one of them by name, accurately and from years back, in the words of a man who has learned to pretend to forget (his exact recall is kept sheathed); (3) how the people see him: no kneeling; voices lowered; a child's stare is not planned; an old petitioner touches the stone of the Wind Crossing as she goes and says that the blood is in a good mood tonight, or a plain phrase of the kind, and nothing of any form is shown; the birds leave the colonnade as he crosses, and a petitioner's dog will not lie down, and he notices every time and it saddens him; (4) the edge and the technique: when the petitioners have gone down the Wind Crossing and the light is going, he goes to the open edge (he seeks altitude after the public self) and draws the greatsword; he shows ***Vigorrondel*** once, at dusk, a cleave with qi gathered from the open air and driven through the blade, shown as a cut and its effect on the evening air and never explained; he sets the blade down two-handed and level and the hands settle (no more than a Ruler's cleave, and no form or part-form); (5) the letter: a pilot of the courier-flights (of the Windward Host's Flightcraft arm, an Airman or a Corporal by grade, called by the bare rank noun or by birth name) lands on the terrace's edge on a wind-coupled glider and gives the letter, with the word that it came from the school at Azorion by the corridor post and waited two days there for a wind that would carry it; the old wire stops at the corridor and the rest of the way is by hand, in one line; the king reads it standing; he takes one deliberate audible breath before the last lines, and the grey of his eyes goes deeper for a second, and the pilot looks away; (6) the boy's letter, as the king reads it: a child of eight in his own words, in short linked sentences of concrete things: the rack, the *First Grip* with a strip of sleeve bound on one by a girl in front of him, the big pupils and the one the pupils call the best, "not yet" given plainly, the ring he wants, and Varya counting everything; no word of the ward, the road or the hurt; no word of his heritage; and at the end one question to his father, how long yet is; (7) the turn: the king holds his son's first letter home, with a question he must answer truthfully, and knows without turning that the queen is coming along the ward-line, late as ever (the end)
- **Continuation:** the scene (S14) continues in Area 04, which changes the viewpoint to Aalia at the same dusk; where this Area ends the king stands at the open edge with the blade lowered and the letter read and in his hand, the pilot has gone out over the edge, the Stone Ring stands empty, and the queen is coming, so the tension stays live (the boy's question is open)
- **Planned time skips:** none
- **Fight (optional):** none
- **Sensory anchor (optional):** unset
- **Tone or voice (optional):** unset (the dialogue standard of section 3 applies: Aeries speaks in long, unhurried, unqualified sentences, and a fragment is either inside the plain humor or a mistake)
- **Content (optional):** unset (row 13: no form, part-form or flight is shown; the king's night flights are not planted; a child's letter shows no harm)

- **Closing type:** turn or reveal: the boy's first letter home ends on his question, how long yet is, a fact shown for the first time, and the Area stops on the king's reaction (the breath, the eyes, the settled hands); the question is put to a father who answers every question truthfully, and it stays open: it is carried into Area 04 and left open when the scene ends, the answer to be given to the boy at a visit or in the holidays, which this Novel-Part does not show (recorded under Part 36 Rule 22.2.6 as left open on purpose).

### Area 04 — The Ward-Walk at Dusk
**Purpose:** The same dusk from the queen's side. Aalia holds the viewpoint. She is late to her walk, as she is late to everything that has a clock, and she walks the ward-line of the palace alone and on foot as the light goes (the palace has no walls, and the wards are its edge). She reads the station data herself, puts right a crooked thing at each station without noticing, and speaks to the keeper of the third station, who says "Madam", exactly as much as was asked. On a long stretch of the line she shows ***Nickvoyage*** once, the advance with the point fixed on the named mark, shown as a woman walking her ward and never as a tool or a calculation. Her house is placed by what she does and never by what she thinks: her husband at the edge, the Stone Vigil's lamps, and the one place in the map that is empty, her son's. She comes to the terrace, takes the letter from the king's hand and reads it; they speak of Azorion, Varys and Valia by habit; the king tells her the boy's question and she answers with a place and not a time. Valia's note of Phase Day 2 is in her mind and unanswered. Dark comes; the house without walls holds; nothing of the raid has reached Gale.
**Want, obstacle, stake:** Aalia wants to see her son grow into himself (her Stated Want), and tonight wants her house whole at the day's end, every person placed and every edge walked. The clock she cannot keep, the falling light and the one gap in her map, her son's empty place, stand in the way. The stake is the small cost of an absent place and the warmth of a friendship kept up by habit. Planned turn: she reads the letter. Planned outcome: a quiet loss and a warmth.
**Viewpoint:** Aalia (hero, Queen of Gale, Ruler Level 1 Mid)
**Dual-POV Area:** no
**Area status:** WRITTEN(1.1.1.005)

**Plan of the Area**
- **Pacing (planned):** Slow, because the Area is a walk, a quiet practice and a talk between two people who know each other (the Part 2 anchor-type defaults for practice and character)
- **Size target:** 3,000 words (a custom figure in a whole Chapter)
- **Budget stretch:** up to 0.72 of target; the draft aims a little under 2,160 words, the cap of 0.72 of target, because the Area is the novel's first sight of the queen: her walk, her way of putting things right, a named technique, a letter read and a talk with her husband in one Slow run
- **Scene anchors:** (1) the late start: the ward-line at dusk, the light already going, the queen coming along it on foot and alone, late as she always is and serene about it; the ward-line is the palace's edge, with stone marks and monitoring stations and no wall; the wind turning to the evening easterly; the Stone Vigil's lamps being lit at the Landing Stone; her interior is imagery and placement and never sentences; (2) the stations: she reads the data of each station herself (the instruments are hers to audit) and puts right what is crooked at each, a latch, a lamp, a pair of boots left at a door, without noticing; at the third station the keeper (Tier D by role) says "Madam" and she answers exactly what is asked and nothing beside it; (3) the technique: on a long stretch of the line she draws the short sword and walks it with the point fixed on the next mark, ***Nickvoyage*** once, shown as a woman walking her ward, with no count, no measure and no named faculty in her viewpoint; (4) the empty place: she passes the door of the room that is her son's, where his other boot has stood by the door since he went, and it is straight (she has straightened it every evening); she does not think a sentence about him; the place is shown by what her hands do; (5) the terrace and the letter: she comes to the open edge where the king stands with the letter in his hand and the blade lowered; she comes to rest beside him, close (her joy shortens distances), takes the letter and reads it; the *First Grip* with a strip of sleeve, the big pupils, "not yet", the ring, Varya counting everything, and the question; (6) the talk: the king tells her the boy's question in his own long, unhurried sentences; she answers with a place where a time was asked, lightly, and it costs nothing; they speak of Azorion, Varys and Valia by habit (Valia's four lines of Phase Day 2 are still unanswered, and she will answer them tomorrow, or the day after); neither of them knows of any raid; (7) the landing: the dark comes, the lamps are lit, the wind holds; the house with no walls stands; they go in (the end)
- **Continuation:** complete scene in this Area, ending the scene S14; where it ends it is dark, the king and the queen go in, the boy's question is held between them and unanswered, and nothing of the raid has reached Gale
- **Planned time skips:** none

### Area 05 — What the Pilot Carried
**Purpose:** The news reaches the Gale court by hand, twice, on Phase Day 14. The Court sits in the open at the Stone Ring in the morning. A pilot of the courier-flights lands from the corridor post with a letter in Varys's own hand, and with the post-master's word standing behind it: the letter was put into the post-master's hand by the Azure Sky king's own rider, he has kept it from that hour, and he will say so before any who ask. The king will not act on a written account until a voice stands behind it, and the pilot is the voice. He reads it standing. It is Varys's own weighing of the raid from before Day One, in the first person, with its own delay named, the confirmed facts and nothing else: the day and place, the number and kind of the raiders, what was taken, the dead and the one who lives, one plain line that a clamp was found on the post's wire at the relay hut by the lower ford and that the post's first dispatch was lost to it, and that the cause is not known; no guess, no source named, no kingdom named and no new event. The king goes rigid and silent for one breath, then takes a full one. Then the Windward Host's Ridgewatch lieutenant, Dagmar Sund, who has waited her turn with a day's mud on her boots, gives her count of what she found on the Seam Road and read from the ground: the empty caravan, the dead, all of them, the escort and the hands, with no one left alive to ask, what the trodden ground says of the raiders and what it does not say, one plain line that the Captain's company is without its standing orders, and why the news is five days old. The two accounts agree in what they describe. The king thanks her by name, asks the one question there is, how the raiders knew the road and the day, holds the letter's one clue beside it with no way to test it at Gale's end, and no one can answer. No promise is made.
**Want, obstacle, stake:** Aeries wants to take in the truth of two raids without letting it move him to a promise he cannot carry for thirty years; he wants, too, to be told plainly by someone who stood on the ground. The paper without a voice, his own hold on himself and a count that has no answer in it stand in the way. The stake is his friendship's plain footing with Varys and the lives of his people on a road he cannot yet read. Planned turn: the lieutenant's count agrees with the letter. Planned outcome: a hard truth taken in; nothing promised.
**Viewpoint:** Aeries (hero, King of Gale, Ruler Level 2 Mid)
**Dual-POV Area:** no
**Area status:** WRITTEN(1.1.1.005)

**Plan of the Area**
- **Pacing (planned):** Medium, because the Area is a letter, a report and a held question told in speech and in the king's stillness (the Part 2 anchor-type defaults for dialogue and information)
- **Size target:** 3,000 words (a custom figure in a whole Chapter)
- **Budget stretch:** up to 0.72 of target; the draft aims a little under 2,160 words, the cap of 0.72 of target, because the Area carries a messenger, a letter, a report and an open question in one Medium run, with the Chapter's second raid told in a lieutenant's own words
- **Scene anchors:** (1) the Court's morning: the Day 14 date line follows the break that opens the scene; the Court sits in the open at the Stone Ring (a descriptor and no named hall) and the king hears a petition in full; the queen stands where the Court's seating puts her; the steward and the Stone Vigil's garrison stand by; the setting is public, so the petitioners and the Court's people say "Your Majesty"; (2) the pilot lands: a pilot of the courier-flights lands at the terrace's edge on a wind-coupled glider (Tier D by role, of the Windward Host's Flightcraft arm, a Senior of the lower Levels, not necessarily the pilot of Area 03) and gives the letter with the post-master's word standing behind it; he names the people who stood at the handing over at the corridor post, so that the king can send and ask them, which is Gale's way of testing a carried word (`gale-kingdom-military` sections 6.8 and 6.11.7) and is what "a voice stands behind it" means here; the letter is a foreign king's letter and no order, so the first-person delivery of an order does not apply; the old wire stops at the corridor and the rest of the way is by hand, in one line; the king stops the hearing and comes across to him (the king who comes to you); (3) the reading: the king reads the letter standing and through; the page gives Varys's weighing in his own manner, plain and in the first person, naming its own delay first (the Court heard of the raid three weeks late, as Chapter 003 holds it, and this letter follows later still), then the confirmed facts of the raid from before Day One and no more, among them, as one plain line and nothing besides, that a clamp was found on the post's wire at the relay hut by the lower ford and that the post's first dispatch was lost to it, which is why the Court heard late (Chapter 003 holds both as confirmed; the letter draws no inference from the clamp, says nothing of who set it and tells none of the Court's guesses or measures), and closing with the question it cannot answer; he goes rigid and silent for one breath, then takes a full one; the Court looks away; his hands settle; the queen moves one step nearer; (4) the lieutenant: Dagmar Sund (Tier D, a Lieutenant of the Windward Host's Ridgewatch detachment), called from the edge of the boards, gives her count in her own words (a count in Gale's sense: evidence of what she saw, which orders nothing and binds nobody, `gale-kingdom-military` section 6.8), in full connected sentences that carry the numbers, in four marked parts: (4a) the finding: Stirrup Ford on the Seam Road on the ninth day, found on the eleventh because the Kite Line does not reach the mine road and her patrol's first ride up the road since the Windward Trail opened to horses was the eleventh, and told only on the fourteenth because the weather stopped the Kite Line on the plateau, so that no summons went ahead of her and she rode to the palace in person; four wagons standing in the ford with the sacks gone and the strongbox open and empty of its papers; Captain Korth and her five dead on the gravel and in the water, named from the Host's roll; the four carters and the clerk dead under and beside the wagons, all five, so that no one lives who saw it; (4b) the ground: what the ground told the patrol, which is all she has, set out in the order she read it and kept to what she saw: the trodden road and bank, about two dozen boots and the track of a train of pack-mules going up the draw above the ford and lost on the shale; the round butt-marks of long hafts in the soft ground at the trees' edge on both banks, some two dozen in all, where a company stood in a line; strips of sacking in the shingle and on the bank's thorn, each torn off in one pull; a torn strip of plain undyed cloth on the third wagon's tailboard and one plain brown coat in the shallows by the lead wheel, without cord, badge or colour, of a kind sold at any market; blood on the shingle that was not Gale's and a dark line of it going up the draw, with none of the raiders' dead left behind, which tells her they carried their own; she says in plain words what the ground gives (what they wore, what they carried, about how many, which way) and what it does not give (a name, a mark, a kingdom), because they left nothing that says it; (4c) the company's orders: one plain line that the Captain's company is without its standing orders, which lived in her alone and went with her, and that no one has yet spoken it new ones, which the king hears and does not answer (left open); (4d) the thanks: the king thanks her by name, and approximates her numbers on purpose, as a courtesy; (5) the likeness and the question: the king (and the queen, who is placed beside him) see that the lieutenant's count and the letter agree in what they describe, plain coats, long hafts bound and wrapped, a company of about two dozen, nothing that names a kingdom; no one says "the same"; the king sets the letter's one hard fact beside his own question, in his thought and not in his speech: someone reached into what was sent on a wire, and he cannot tell whether anyone has reached into what Gale sends, because Gale's end of the wire is absent (the old wire stops at the corridor, and the letter came the last way by hand), so the clue cannot be tested here and he follows it no further; nobody names a source, a band or a kingdom; the weather and pressure words surface in his speech once, when he says the air has gone flat, because something has alarmed him; he asks the one question there is, how did they know the road and the day, and no one can answer, the lieutenant least of all (the end)
- **Continuation:** the scene (S15) continues in Area 06, which changes the viewpoint to Aalia at the same moment; where this Area ends the Court stands in the open at the Stone Ring, the letter lies read on the Court's table, the lieutenant has finished and stands where she gave her count, the king has asked his question and no one has answered, and no one has promised anything, so the tension stays live
- **Planned time skips:** none
- **Fight (optional):** none (the raid is reported and never shown again; the report gives only what the ground gives, and every line of it is read against the Engagement Ledgers of Areas 01 and 02: about two dozen of the company where the ledgers hold twenty-four, the escort of six and the five hands dead, the company's dead carried out, the ore and the manifest taken; Part 44 Rule 44.9.2.1)
- **Sensory anchor (optional):** unset
- **Tone or voice (optional):** unset (the dialogue standard of section 3 applies: the lieutenant's report is full connected sentences and never a tally; Aeries's alarm register surfaces once)
- **Content (optional):** unset (the dead are named and counted in a lieutenant's own words, with no gore beyond what Gate 1 allows; no source, no band and no kingdom is named by any character)

**Opening, closing and connection**
- **Opening type:** time jump: two days on from Day 12 to Day 14, in the same place (the Aerie Palace) and the same line, as a new scene with its own date line; the Court's morning, the open terrace and a pilot landing in the first lines carry the jump; no step back.
- **Closing type:** turn or reveal: the two accounts agree and the king asks his one question; the Area stops on the reaction of the king and the queen, with the explanation left for the next Area; the question stays open and is carried into Area 06.
- **Carry-over from the previous Area:** Area 04 closed on Day 12 at dark with the king and the queen going in, the boy's question held between them, and nothing of the raid yet known to Gale. Since then two days have passed without the page. The reader knows the raid of Day 9 in full and the raid before Day One; the Gale court knows neither. Valia's four lines of Phase Day 2 are still unanswered.

### Area 06 — Two Seals
**Purpose:** The same moment from the queen's side, then the afternoon. Aalia places the Court after the report, asks the lieutenant the one question she can ask, a question of where, and takes the answer away unspoken; she arranges food and rest for the lieutenant and a seat for the pilot, and the Court rises. The king goes to write to Varys and she goes to write to Valia. The king's letter tells Varys of the raid of Phase Day 9, plainly and in full, in Gale's way. It takes the clamp as a plain fact and says that Gale has no wire for one to be set on. He reads it aloud once to the hearers he names before he seals it, so that the record is the people who heard it. It binds nothing: no war is declared, no joint action is set, no exchange between the courts is begun, and the Pause that comes before any promise is kept. The queen's letter answers Valia's four lines of Phase Day 2 and tells her friend the news, the one friend she tells everything; Aegon's absence is in both answers, in the king's thanks for the boy's bed in Varys's house and in the queen's word of the boy's boot. The two seals are set in two ways in two rooms: the king after the long pause between reading and sealing, the queen when the air moves. The question of how the raiders found out stands on both desks, unanswered.
**Want, obstacle, stake:** Aalia wants to answer her friend, in the one voice she has, and to place her house after two blows without arranging it into a quiet it has not earned. Her own register (she gives a conclusion and has no reasoning to give), her husband's Pause and a question with no where in it stand in the way. The stake is the friendship, which is the alliance's emotional centre, and her house's whole footing. Planned turn: the two seals. Planned outcome: a warm, binding-nothing answer sent; the question held.
**Viewpoint:** Aalia (hero, Queen of Gale, Ruler Level 1 Mid)
**Dual-POV Area:** no
**Area status:** WRITTEN(1.1.1.005)

**Plan of the Area**
- **Pacing (planned):** Medium, because the Area is a short exchange, a decision to write and two letters sealed (the Part 2 anchor-type defaults for dialogue and character)
- **Size target:** 3,000 words (a custom figure in a whole Chapter)
- **Budget stretch:** up to 0.72 of target; the draft aims a little under 2,160 words, the cap of 0.72 of target, because the Area carries a question, two letters and two seals, and closes the Chapter, in one Medium run
- **Scene anchors:** (1) the Court placed: in motion from the last moment of Area 05, the queen at the Court one step nearer the king than before; she places the Court as she would a table (the lieutenant on the boards with her mud, the pilot to one side, the steward and the garrison); her hands put right a crooked thing on the Court's table without her noticing; her interior is imagery and placement and never sentences; (2) her one question: she asks Dagmar Sund where, and in a form the lieutenant can answer completely: where the Captain fell; the answer is plain and full (on the gravel bar between the strongbox wagon and the third wagon, with the water at her back and the stones before her dark where the raiders had fallen, as the reader saw her stand in Area 01), and the queen takes it away and says nothing of what she makes of it (the conclusion, if she has one, is absent and not withheld; no reasoning is given or sought); in her thought she leaves unfilled the hole where the Captain's company's orders had been, because the filling is not hers to do until the king has spoken it, standing, before named witnesses; optionally the answer lets the reader see that both raids lie on roads below a ford, with no comment (an optional beat); (3) the Court rises: she arranges food and rest for the lieutenant and a seat for the pilot, steering and not instructing; the Court's people go down the Wind Crossing; (4) the king's letter: the king goes to a table at the open side of the terrace to write; she is placed where she can see his hands and not his words; when he has written he reads it aloud once, in his long, unhurried sentences, before the hearers he names (the queen, the steward and the pilot who will carry it), so that the record is the people who heard it: he tells Varys of the raid of Phase Day 9 in plain facts (the dead to the last, what the ground gave, the ore and the manifest taken, and the question), thanks him for telling him and for keeping the boy in his house, takes the clamp as a plain fact, says that Gale has no wire for a clamp to be set on and that Gale's officers will walk Gale's road, and binds nothing; (5) the two rooms: he goes in with the letter read and unsealed, to take the long pause that Gale's custom puts between reading and sealing; she goes to her own rooms; ⁂ about two hours pass while she writes; (6) the queen's letter: in her own rooms she writes to Valia, in short sentences that give the answer first; she answers the four lines of Phase Day 2 (the note itself is never shown) and tells her friend the news that she can tell no one else; she tells her of the boy's boot; (7) the two seals: she waits for the air to move before she seals, and the king's pause ends in the other room at nearly the same moment, shown by what the house does and never by a faculty named in her viewpoint; the king's seal is set after his pause and the queen's when the air moves, two ways of sealing in two rooms, the queen at her window seeing the pilot take both letters on the terrace and put them inside his coat together, and the wind turns; the question of how the raiders found out lies under the wax of each, unanswered (the end of the Chapter)
- **Continuation:** complete scene in this Area, ending the scene S15 and the Chapter; where it ends both letters are sealed and in the pilot's hands, taken on the terrace (the king's from his own hand, the queen's from the boy's), the queen at her window in her own rooms, and the question is open
- **Planned time skips:** after anchor 5, about two hours, while the king takes his pause and the queen writes her letter in her own rooms (shown with ⁂)
