## 6 — Completion Record

Written at completion (Rule 3.7.1) on 2026-10-19, in the same pass as the Gate 2 sync of the whole build (Rule 3.6.1, the whole-Chapter paragraph). The completion answers were recorded on 2026-10-20.

**Final Variance Summary:**

Whole Chapter, built whole by `/create-chapter` (Build mode whole) and written at the Gate 2 of 2026-10-19 after the author's `[CONFIRM WRITE]`. Planned and written: six Area blocks in three scenes (S13, S14 and S15) on three days, Year 1247, Ilovany, Nuvil, Day 9 (Phase Day 9), Alovil, Day 12 (Phase Day 12) and Mavil, Day 14 (Phase Day 14), in the viewpoints of Sigrun Korth (Area 01), Wulfric Rask (Area 02), Aeries (Areas 03 and 05) and Aalia (Areas 04 and 06), in the order the ambush at Stirrup Ford, the same ambush from the raid leader's side, the king's evening at the open edge, the queen's ward-walk, the pilot's letter with the lieutenant's count, and the two seals. No Area block was added, split, merged, dropped or moved, no Area is a Dual-POV Area and no Area file exists. Sizes against targets, in words (counting method: a word is a whitespace token that holds a word character; date lines, hidden Area markers and break marks are not counted; the figures are the Maker's, given for the final text by the orchestrating command, the script `measure` being the figure of record, and this lane ran no script, since it holds no shell): Area 01 2,124 of 3,000 (70.8 percent); Area 02 1,866 (62.2); Area 03 2,083 (69.4); Area 04 1,707 (56.9); Area 05 2,052 (68.4); Area 06 1,702 (56.7); Chapter 11,534 of T = 18,000 (64.1 percent; band 9,900 to 12,960). Every Area is inside the 55 to 72 percent band its Budget stretch names (the lowest is Area 06 at 56.7 percent and the highest Area 01 at 70.8), and the Chapter is inside its band, 1,426 words under the ceiling the draft was written to, so no band exception was needed. Pacing is carried as planned (Fast, Fast, Slow, Slow, Medium, Medium). The Scene Map was met: S13 holds 3,990 words, S14 3,790 and S15 3,754, each against a cap of 4,320 (information). Read against the Area band of canon v10.0 (1,280 to 2,400 words per Area), which a whole-built Chapter does not apply, and as information only: all six Areas fall inside it. Nothing is asked and no block follows from it.

What the Novel-Part entry fixes, top-down. The departures from the entry were planned at the build, and the author's answer at the confirm was to leave the entry as the plan baseline, with variance notes only (Settled, 2026-10-18); the notes of that date are on Section Spec 1.1 and Novel-Part Spec 1.1.1. As written, the Chapter meets what the entry fixes: the purpose by function name (the early raid, Gale on its own ground, Aeries and Aalia with one named technique each, the alliance by traces, Gale's end of the wire by absence); the calendar window (Day 9 to Day 14, three date lines); the threads in and out; who is on the page (the viewpoints in the order Korth, Rask, Aeries, Aalia, Aeries, Aalia, and none of the eight protagonists on the page in the raid); the key events; the reveals owed (the reader alone knows whose company it was, and neither court learns a source or how the raiders found the road); the opening hook; and the closing beat, which the Chapter goes one image beyond (D10). Information notes were appended at this Gate 2 to Section Spec 1.1 and then Novel-Part Spec 1.1.1 (the closing image, the first-use clause in the queen's thought, the Tier D persons named at the writing and, on the Novel-Part Spec, the Chapter word budget), with one Audit note on each, and the Coverage cells of the two registry rows were refreshed in the same write (Part 38 Rule 3.6.1, the whole-Chapter paragraph; Rule 1.2.2). Neither parent is completed: Novel-Part Spec 1.1.1 still has four Chapter entries without a Chapter Spec, and a Novel-Part Spec and a Section Spec are completed at their own boundary, on the author's yes.

Differences between the plan and the text (D1 to D13), recorded from the Maker's hand-off and from this lane's reading of the written text. The Gate 1 plan-versus-written list was not among this lane's inputs; the author's `[CONFIRM WRITE]` of the Chapter as it stands accepted the listed differences (Part 38 Rule 6.1.1(e)), and the items marked "author-ruled" carry a ruling the orchestrating command relayed. The full notes, with the planned wording beside the written wording, are in the Audit and Settlement Notes:

- Area 02 (D1): the Area opens live on Rask at the trees' edge, reading the gravel bar in the last moments before he goes down to it, and gives the count-in and the sign straight after as recollection in two short paragraphs (plan: in motion from the last moment of Area 01, a few breaths back to the sign).
- Area 03 (D2): the descent through the king's senses is three paragraphs (plan: one paragraph or two).
- Area 03 (D3): the hearing that runs past its hour is not rendered as a cost, so the first payment of Aeries's `B39-L36-COST` is not on the page; the author deferred the payment (to a later Chapter, as the Open note of the build set out).
- Area 03 (D4): Aeries's silver-black hair is the first physical detail of him on the page (information; it equals the ledger's own line).
- Area 04 (D5): "She came to rest beside him, close", with her shoulder against his arm at the end of the talk, in place of the planned "a step nearer than she would stand to a stranger" (author-ruled).
- Area 04 (D6): one memory the plan does not list: at the second station, a boy of five on the smooth place of the mark who would not go on until she told him what the stone was for ("the edge of here"); a place-first memory in her viewpoint, not a scene (information).
- Areas 04 and 06 (D7): Aalia's "gate" lines are her private intent; no journey, date, plan or promise follows (information).
- Area 05 (D8): the eyes beat of Area 03 is not repeated; the Court looks away instead (a staging difference).
- Area 06 (D9): the queen's thought carries the clause "until the king had spoken it, standing, before named witnesses", which the author kept on purpose (author-ruled).
- Area 06 (D10): the Chapter ends with the pilot taking both letters on the terrace, the king's from his own hand and the queen's from the boy's, seen by the queen from her window (plan: both letters in the pilot's hands and the king and the queen in two rooms).
- Areas 03 to 06 (D11): the writing named nine Tier D persons of Gale beyond the author's seven, and two of them, the Stone Vigil's sentries, carry a worded level that the plan did not give (the drafting pass's picks; information).
- Areas 03, 05 and 06 (D12): the Court's Day 14 hearing is the drain case set before the Court in Area 03, so two petitioners stand again in Areas 05 and 06, their speech reported and never quoted (information).
- All Areas (D13): the first date line stands below the Area 01 marker, as in Chapter 003, where Chapter 004 put the line above its marker (information).

Rulings in force at the write (all the author's, 2026-10-18 unless dated otherwise, none re-asked): the title kept; the Court held on the open terrace as a descriptor, with the Court's stones named the Stone Ring; the seven named Gale people Tier D, with the deaths of the escort and of the five hands ruled as planned; Dagmar Sund a Lieutenant; no one of the caravan lives; the loss of the Captain's company's standing orders told as one plain line and left open; Aeries's technique at dusk on the open edge; Varys's letter carrying the wire clamp as one plain confirmed fact; the ground-reading the Gale patrol gives; the information behind the ambush named as a kind of source only; Hadric Stoll riding as a squad leader with the stage of his Level 6 unset; Wulfric Rask ending the captain; the address ruling ("Sire" and "Madam" in private, "Your Majesty" in public, the bare rank noun for a soldier); the two collective codes, THE OPEN EDGE and THE RECITAL; the answer to leave the Novel-Part entry as the plan baseline with variance notes only; and, at Gate 1 and the write (relayed 2026-10-19), the clause of D9 kept, the wording of D5 and the deferral of D3.

Check #21 (Gate 1): the Maker's hand-off declares no behaviour change and lists five information items: the technique shown at dusk on the open edge in place of the registered late-night session (the author's ruled variation); the hearing that runs past its hour not rendered as a cost, since the author deferred the payment; Aalia's hands squaring the letter, which curls and is squared again, so that nothing is left crooked and her drift row 3 is not engaged; the weather word "flat" spoken once in alarm, as registered; and no Tier D person changing. The Gate 1 checker's own rulings were not among this lane's inputs, so none is restated here; any ruling is written to the character files by the ledger lane.

Audit coverage. Gate 1 ran a first full audit and a re-audit of the Chapter text (the Maker's hand-off revision note names both), and the author confirmed the write. The Gate 2 audit lane (read-only) and the ledger lane (lane 2b) report separately, and their findings are not restated here. This lane (lane 2c) wrote this spec, the three registry rows and the notes on the two parent specs.

Completion answers (asked at `/chapter-spec complete`; the author's answers were given in chat on 2026-10-20 and relayed by the orchestrating command at the confirm of Chapter Spec 1.1.1.006). The Chapter is complete (status COMPLETED already). Differences D1 to D13 are accepted as written. Every Open, Deferred and unpaid item is carried forward and none is lapsed except the two the author lapsed: the keeper Ragna Holm and the queen's lateness (texture, lapsed on the author's answer). Carried: the stage of Hadric Stoll's Level 6; the map ruling for Gale's northern frontier with Brackenhold; the ledger text the address ruling supersedes; the payment of Aeries's `B39-L36-COST`; the Captain's company's standing orders; Aegon's "how long yet"; the third line of Valia's note, answered at the gate; the wire clamp; The Lone Banner; when either court learns whose raiders struck; how the raiders found the two caravans; the rivals' aims and rulers; the village's land (now answered at Chapter Spec 1.1.1.006: the Azure Sky ground stands, the land-holder is unset, and the village chief is titled Village Chief); and the pressure answer (decided per Chapter). Eleven items were carried to Chapter Spec 1.1.1.006, among them the village's land, the pressure answer and the beasts' Kind, which were answered there. The seeds and the digest above are unchanged.

**Reader now knows:**

What the page of Chapter 005 has shown, by Area block (the Chapter reference is 1.1.1.005 throughout). It seeds the next Carry-over.

- Area 01. On the morning of Year 1247, Ilovany, Nuvil, Day 9, with mist on the stream and the low road, Sigrun Korth (***Warrior Level 7 High***), a captain of the Windward Host, stops four wagons of Whistledeep ore (low-grade Power Crystal in sacks, bound for the Aerie Artificers' College) on the shale ledge above Stirrup Ford on the Seam Road, as she stops every file. She speaks her standing orders aloud and whole, as at every halt (cross by daylight and in one file; the strongbox wagon rides in the middle with Ulm on its bench; the horn sounded once and never twice; the carters answered for before a sack; a file does not turn back on a feeling), and places her five by name and Level: Torvek Gant (fourth Level, high), Yrsa Wold (fourth, middle), Jorund Ulm (fourth, low), Ingrith Mott (third, the top of it) and Egil Hurn (third, middle). Four carters and a clerk ride with the file. Gant lays a palm on each wheel hub; Wold draws the cutting, the bar and the far bank in the shale dust before she speaks; Ulm never leaves the strongbox bench; Mott talks to the mules; Hurn laughs, short and early. Her sense finds nothing across the water, no bird calls, and the Trail has been open to horses only days; a man in a good brown coat walks away along the ditch with his hands in his sleeves, and she gives him one look and no more. The wagons enter the ford. The qi comes all at once, seventeen weights round the file (six of the fifth Level, low and middle; nine of the fourth, middle and high; two near her own, one a little under her and one further under), and halberds come out of their sacking in a single pull (***Brawnhaft***). The lead guards go down in the mist: Gant at the lead wheel, a carter hauled from his seat and finished, Hurn cut off. She sounds the horn once and nothing answers; the nearest ear is the Ridgewatch outpost at the foot of the Mule Stair. She steps onto the gravel bar between the strongbox wagon and the third with the water at her back and holds the two leaders and the line; one man goes into the shallows and is hauled out of his coat by his own side, and another's sleeve hangs from the third wagon's tailboard. The mist cuts her off from her five, a carter's cry and the clerk's end, and Hurn's laugh stops. A cold ache comes into her knees, a weight far above her stands along the near bank where the trees begin, and something walks out of the trees.
- Area 02. The same moment from the trees. Rask (***Warrior Level 9 Peak***), qi drawn in to nothing, reads the gravel bar from the trees' edge in the last moments before he goes down to it: the captain is a steady weight a little above anything else there, two of the centre rank lie at her feet and a third goes down while he reads. The count-in is given as recollection. Before the light he stood with the company along the bank, the long hafts in their sacking planted butt-down in the soft ground, and counted the file in with his lips shut (six who would fight, a captain among them, and four carters and a clerk who would not); twenty-three stood under his hand, seventeen on this bank and six across the stream where the rear rank crossed in the dark, and himself the twenty-fourth; the two leaders at the sixth Level and the seventh, low; the first rank at the fifth, low and middle; the centre rank at the fourth, middle and high; the rear rank at the third, high, and the fourth, low; the pack-mules waited in the draw with their muzzles bound; Brackenhold's king had asked for this road in few words, and Rask carried the words exactly. His right hand rose to the height of his shoulder and came down once to send the first rank (the wraps tore from six hafts in one long sound, ***Brawnhaft***), a second lift sent the centre, and no word started them. He counted eleven heartbeats of silence after the horn, with the outpost far down the stair by his choice of hour, and gave the order low, in full sentences with no reason in them. Hadric Stoll, a squad leader of the sixth Level, repeats the order for his squad in ordered steps and works the captain's left side with ***Staticsnatch***, slower and more exactly each time; the captain cuts nine times, and the ninth is a hand's width slower than the first. At the strongbox wagon the last three hold on (***Falsedge Barrier***, ***Yelmanprop***, ***Whirlflog***) and two of the centre rank fall there. When the lead is clean Rask walks down alone, the only walking on that bank, and his weight reaches the captain first; she is still speaking her orders ("We cross by daylight and in one file") when the hook goes in behind her knee (***Subjugating Hookback***) and the edge comes down once. He does not look at what he has done. The other leader's ***Rubble Axefall*** takes a wheel rim; ***Axefall Decree*** sweeps the bar, the mist peels off the stream in a single skin, and the last three of the escort go down (the woman among the mules says "Easy, grey, easy, it is only water, and I am here"); he leans on the haft for a count of three. Five of the centre rank lie dead, three more are bound with cloth and one is whole. The pack-mules come down, the sacks go to their backs, the strongbox straps are undone at the buckle and not slashed, and Stoll puts a flat book and a sheaf of papers inside his coat. The carters and the clerk are dead, the fifth found under the third wagon, and the count closes at five and none left, with no cry and no name on the road; the ground is gathered and called clean. His own fallen go up the draw first, five on the shoulders of ten; his right hand, raised and held, is the sign to leave; he goes last with the haft for a staff and does not look toward the edge of the mist where the ditch ran out of the cutting. A bird calls twice on the far bank.
- Area 03. Year 1247, Ilovany, Alovil, Day 12, the evening, three days on and with no word of the raid. Aeries (***Ruler Level 2 Mid***) reads the evening wind from the open edge of the terrace of a palace with no walls: the long press along the foot of the Windward Range; the terraced fields; the masts of the *Kite Line* whistling on their kite-chains, all but the last two to the north, silent under the weather on the Range; the road down to Highgale, an hour at a rider's pace to the east-south-east; and the city's eight districts, each met by one thing read in it (Frame Row, Rack Market, Courier Green, Muster Field, Leeside, Weighhouse Row, Elders' Rise and the Ropewalk). The palace's only edge is the ward-line, a ring of stone marks and watch stations carved in the pale stone of the rise, from the Landing Stone, where the kingdom had been sworn, round to the Wind Crossing, the stone causeway that brings petitioners up from the plateau road; the Stone Ring stands empty, the tall stones in a circle on the terrace where the Court takes up the kingdom's business in the open; the wind lifts his silver-black hair and finds him wanting more sky than the terrace gives. The steward Ranulf Skeld, who has no qi in him to read, says the Court has risen and the last two petitioners will not go down the Crossing until they have said it to the king's face; the king says a man who has waited since the noon bell has been in the Court the whole time he waited, and goes across to them himself. As he crosses, the birds go out of the row of pillars together and a grey herd dog with one folded ear will not lie down. Ottar Haug has a terrace wall down across his field and a neighbour, Knut Dahl, who says it fell of its own weakness; the king lets Haug tell all of it, asks what stood under the drain Dahl dug above the wall, and sets Dahl before the Court on the morning of Mavil, then the two of them to look at the wall together. Hallgerd Vorn's business is a long stair to Rack Market with no rope; he asks after her grandson Torkel, home from the Long Coast nine winters ago, and she is astonished that he remembers; Ranulf will walk her stair in the morning. Nothing is written: the steward and the Stone Vigil's watch at the Landing Stone are the record. Neither petitioner knelt; at the first stone of the Crossing Hallgerd lays her palm flat on it and tells it that the blood is in a good mood tonight, and the king stands with his back to them and presses his hands together until they are still. At the open edge, in the going light, he draws the greatsword and cuts once, ***Vigorrondel***, a long clean seam in the air that the birds bank away from and the nearest mast breaks its whistle for; he sets the blade out level in both hands, and his hands press once on the grip and settle. A glider lands: Corporal Ivar Lund of the Flightcraft arm (a Senior of the lower Levels) brings a letter from the prince at the school, which lay two days at the corridor post waiting for a wind; the old wire stops at the corridor and the rest was by hand. Aegon's letter, a child's, leaning to the right: Uncle Varys says he may write what he likes and Aunt Valia says to start with the rack; the rack has sixty wooden swords and Varya counted them twice; the *First Grip* goes on your fist so the sword does not turn, and his is blue and he put it on upside down; a girl called Brea tore a strip off her own sleeve with her teeth and bound hers on; the big pupils fight in rings of white sand, and the best talks the whole time; five of them fought him at once and nobody won; Varya asked a big girl called Kerra if she could try, and Kerra said not yet, as plain as the time; and, last, "I want the ring. I am not sad, I am only telling you I want it. Varya counts everything and says a time with no length is half a sentence. Father, how long yet is?" The king smiles at the blue cover and at the girl's teeth, and at Kerra's two words the smile goes; he draws one slow breath that carries across the flags, the pilot finds something to look at beyond the edge, and the grey goes deeper in his eyes for a second. The true answer to the boy is an age, and he has been buying the years before it one winter at a time. He folds the page along the boy's creases. The first of the Stone Vigil's lamps catches at the Landing Stone, and along the ward-line the air breaks around someone walking it, late and calm. He does not turn.
- Area 04. The same dusk. Aalia (***Ruler Level 1 Mid***) comes late to the ward-line, having agreed the hour that morning and not kept the agreement through the afternoon; the Vigil's lamp at the Landing Stone is lit before she reaches the first station. The line is pale marks, each a pillar worn smooth on its windward side by the keepers' hands, with a low stone hut like a loaf on a shelf against it; the keepers light their lamps for the queen's hour and not for the sun. At the first station she drops a loose latch into its catch and reads the dial and the slate; at the second she sets a crooked lamp chimney square, and the smooth place on the mark beside it holds a memory of her son at five asking what the stone was for ("the edge"; the edge of what; "the edge of here"); at the third the keeper Ragna Holm, an unawakened woman with chalk on both cuffs, says "Madam" and that the needle has stood a little high since the wind came round, and the queen turns the keeper's boots until the toes agree and tells her to open the glass after the lamps are lit. Beyond the third station, over open ground, she draws the short sword and walks to the mark the keepers call Elbow with the point fixed on it, ***Nickvoyage***, and lays her free hand flat on the stone. At the family wing the last door before the terrace stair is her son's, open on a dark room, and his other boot stands by it, straight already; she straightens it anyway. On the terrace the Vigil changes its watch without looking her way; Aeries stands at the open edge with the greatsword's point on the flags and a folded letter; she comes to rest beside him, close, and he puts it in her hand. She reads it with one finger, smoothing each fold, and the finger stops where her son has not lost his boot. He asks what Aegon asks, how long yet is, and when they should go to him; she says "We will go to the gate." He laughs, low, the plain laugh, and says it is the first answer anyone has put in his hand that day that he could walk to; they speak of Varys standing in that gate since the morning, of Valia finding him a chair, of Varya having torn her own sleeve by now and counted the threads first, and of the letter that lay two days at the corridor post (Valia would say that is what a wire is for). He asks after Valia's note, under the blue bowl by the window; she will answer it tomorrow, or the day after. She stands nearer, until her shoulder is against his arm. Dark comes: the Vigil's lamps, the stations as small yellow windows, the lamps of Highgale district by district, the Range a darker dark with weather sitting on it and its masts silent. She puts the page in the breast of his coat, and they go in together, past doors that stand open on the flags, to a house with no walls and every place in it kept but one.
- Area 05. Year 1247, Ilovany, Mavil, Day 14, the morning. The Court sits in the open at the Stone Ring, on the Court's boards (a plank floor inside the stones where whoever speaks stands), and Aeries sits on the king's seat, a block of the same pale stone. Knut Dahl, a stout unawakened man with a drain drawn in charcoal on a slate, argues his drain; it comes out that a spring runs below Haug's wall and that Haug's father once complained of a wet corner; Ottar Haug waits with his grey dog close against his leg. Aalia stands at the king's left hand, where the Court's seating puts her; Gudrun Nyland (a Senior of the middle Levels) and Leif Bakke (a Senior of the upper Levels) stand watch for the Stone Vigil at the Landing Stone; a woman of the Windward Host waits with a day's mud on her boots. A glider comes down at the terrace's edge: Sverre Vik, an Airman of the Flightcraft arm (a Senior of the lower Levels), holds out a sealed letter in both hands and waits to be asked. The king crosses to him. Vik says it is the king of Azure Sky's letter in his own writing, that Marta Kell, post-master at the corridor post, took it from the king's own rider and put it in his hands before her clerk Ewan Mabry and a groom of the post, and that they will say so to anyone sent to ask; the king will have Ranulf send to ask, and reads it now. Varys's letter owes the lateness first and says what he knows: on the eighth day of Ulenor at dusk a caravan of five wagons from the Bluecleft mine was taken on the lower road below the lower ford by about twenty men in plain coats, with halberds and no mark of any kingdom, every one a Warrior; they took the mine's two head chests; four of his are dead (Davin Tarn, Wil Harker and Edric Gower on the road, and the mine's clerk, Perrin Lisle, in the hospital in the night); Ansel Roke, who led the guard, is alive and mending; a clamp was found fixed to the wire under the floor of the relay hut at the lower ford, and the first report of the raid was lost to it, which is why the Court heard so late; he does not know who put it there, who they were, or how they knew the road and the hour, and he will not guess. Aeries does not move for one breath, then takes a full one and his hands settle; the queen leaves her place by one step. Dagmar Sund (***Senior Level 9 High***), Lieutenant of the Ridgewatch detachment, gives her count as a count, which orders nothing: the caravan from Whistledeep was taken at Stirrup Ford on the ninth; her patrol found it on the eleventh, on its first ride up the Mule Stair and the Seam Road since the Windward Trail opened to horses, because the *Kite Line* does not reach the mine road; four wagons stand in the ford with the sacks gone and the strongbox open and empty of its papers; Captain Korth and her five lie dead on the gravel and in the water (she names them from the Host's roll) and the four carters and the clerk under and beside the wagons, eleven in all, so that no one is left who saw it; the weather came down on the Range the night they found them and stopped the *Kite Line* at the plateau's north edge, so she rode to the king herself and he hears it on the fourteenth. Then the ground: about two dozen men stood in the trees on both banks (the round butt-marks of long hafts in a row, and about as many boot-tracks); a train of pack-mules came down the draw and went up it again, lost on the shale; strips of sacking lie in the gravel and on the thorn, each torn off in a single pull; a torn strip of plain undyed cloth hangs on the third wagon's tailboard and one plain brown coat lay in the shallows by the lead wheel, with no cord, badge or colour, of a kind sold at any market in the kingdom; there was blood that was not Gale's and a dark trail of it up the draw, with none of the raiders' dead left behind. It gives what they wore, what they carried, about how many and which way, and not a name, a mark or a kingdom. Last, Captain Korth carried her company's standing orders in herself and spoke them at every halt, and nowhere were they written; they went into the water with her, and no one has yet spoken her company new ones. The king thanks the lieutenant by name, gives her figures back, and does not answer the last matter. The two accounts agree and no one on the boards says so; he sets the one hard thing in the letter, the clamp, beside her count and follows it as far as it goes, which is not far: someone reached into what was sent along a wire, Gale has no wire for anyone to reach into, and he cannot tell whether anyone has reached into what Gale sends. He says the air has gone flat, tells the lieutenant why so that she does not think the fault is in her report, and asks, "How did they know the road, and how did they know the day?" Nobody answers, and the queen does not go back to her place.
- Area 06. The same moment from the queen's side. Aalia places the Court as she would lay a table after bad news (the lieutenant with her mud and her tiredness, the pilot to one side with his hands empty, the steward, Nyland and Bakke at their stone, Dahl and his drain gone small at the edge, Haug's dog pressed flat to the planks) and squares the curled corner of Varys's letter on the Court's table twice. Two new places have come into her, one under the Range and one on a road she has never walked, and they fit no shape she owns. She asks her one question: "Where did the Captain fall?" Dagmar Sund answers that it was on the gravel bar between the strongbox wagon and the third with the water at her back, and that the stones in front of her were dark all across where the raiders fell. The queen takes the answer away and says nothing of it: she puts the bar beside the road below the lower ford that the letter names ("Two fords."), and she leaves the hole where the Captain's company's orders had been unfilled, because the filling was not hers to do until the king had spoken it, standing, before named witnesses. The king rises the Court and says he will write to Varys now and would have the pilot wait; the steward says it after him, naming the day, and Nyland and Bakke say it after the steward, so that it stands in four mouths. The queen says none of what wants saying: a hand on the steward's sleeve and a boy sent to see that the lieutenant eats, sleeps and is let alone; a glance to the clerks' table so that the Host's roll is copied out for the families before the sun is down (counts are written in Gale, and it is only promises that are not); a stool turned toward the sun and a bowl in the pilot's hands. The petitioners go down the Wind Crossing, Haug's dog first. The king writes at a plain plank table on trestles at the open side of the terrace, his left hand flat on the sheet while the wind passes; she stands where she sees his hands and not his words. He names the hearers, as the custom is (the queen, Ranulf Skeld and Sverre Vik, who will carry it), and reads the letter once, aloud, in his long and unhurried voice. It tells Varys of the raid on the ninth (about two dozen men in plain coats with long hafts and no mark that gives a name; six soldiers of the Windward Host dead, a captain among them, and four carters and a clerk, eleven in all; no one lives who saw it; the ore and the papers taken and the wagons left standing in the water); takes the clamp as Varys gave it, as a fact, and follows it no further; says that Gale has no wire for a clamp to be fixed to, that he cannot say how the raiders knew Gale's road and day, and that he is putting Varys's question back in nearly the same words; says that Gale's officers will walk Gale's road; and thanks Varys for telling him and for keeping his son in his house, boot and all. The paper is only the talk set down, and the record is the three who heard it; when he finishes she is a step nearer than she had been. He leaves it unsealed for the long pause that Gale's custom puts between the reading of a thing and its seal, tells the pilot to sit in the sun, and takes the letter in; she does not follow him, and goes to her own rooms. After one marked skip (`* * *`), two hours on, Aalia has written one page to Valia, answer first: yes to the first line, yes to the second, the third she will answer at the gate because she cannot say it in a letter, yes to the fourth; then the news (on the ninth eleven of ours were killed at a ford under the Range, six soldiers, four carters and a clerk, and nobody lives; a lieutenant who rode three days told it; Varys's letter came in the same hour, and she has put the two side by side; "I do not know yet what I see. Something has moved and I cannot say what. When I can, I will say it to you first."; Aeries has written to Varys, it binds nothing, and neither does this; Aegon wrote on the eighth and has one boot with him, the other stands by his door, and she straightens it every evening, and it is already straight). She will not seal into still air; she warms the wax twice, and hears a latch lift, a chair pushed back, a step, a door and a stool scraping on the terrace; the curtain moves, the air comes from the east and then the south, and she presses the seal down in it. From her window she sees Sverre Vik take the two letters on the terrace, hers from the boy's hand and the king's from the king's own, look from the one seal to the other and put them inside his coat together; they will go by one hand and one road to the corridor, where the wire begins, with the question under the wax of each and no answer in either. The wind has turned.
- Not touched by the page: whose company struck either caravan (no Gale person names a source, a band or a kingdom, and the man in the good coat is never heard of in Gale); how the raiders found either caravan; the matter the author holds about Brackenhold (nothing is shown or hinted); any form, part-form or flight of the king or the queen (row 13); Aeries's night office and night flight and the open-ring promise (all dropped from this Chapter); the cost of Aeries's reachability (deferred); Aegon's heritage (his letter names none); what the new orders of the Captain's company are, who gives them and when; and whether and when the king and the queen go to Azorion (the gate is a place and no plan).
- Seeds for the next Carry-over, kept here because nothing may survive only in working files:
  - (1) The clock and the threads. The story clock stands on Year 1247, Ilovany, Mavil, Day 14 (Phase Day 14) at the close of the Chapter; the calendar ledger owns the recorded date. Both courts now hold both raids: the Azure Sky court has Ansel Roke's question and its three guesses (Chapter 003), and the Gale court has Varys's letter and Dagmar Sund's count; neither court knows whose men they were; the clamp is the one clue, and Gale cannot test it. The two answers, the king's (read aloud once before three hearers) and the queen's, leave in the pilot's coat for the corridor post, and they bind nothing. Chapter 006 opens on a plain change of line to the border village at dusk (the Novel-Part's cut plan, row 005), and is planned in Melorany (Phase Day 29 to Phase Day 59), so a gap follows.
  - (2) What Gale knows and does not. Gale knows about two dozen men in plain coats with long hafts and no mark, a company that carries its own dead and leaves a track that ends on the shale, a clamp on a wire, a hole where a Captain's company's orders had been, and one question. It knows nothing of a king of Brackenhold, the Redhaft Vanguard (the page of this Chapter never says that name), the man in the good coat or Wulfric Rask. The reader alone knows whose company it was.
  - (3) Address as used. Gale people say "Your Majesty" to the king and to the queen in the public setting of the open terrace (the steward, the petitioners, the pilots and the lieutenant); "Madam" is said once, by the keeper of the third ward station to the queen at dusk on the ward-line; "Captain" (Korth's people to her) and "Lieutenant" (the king to Dagmar Sund, who names herself "Lieutenant of the Ridgewatch detachment") are bare rank nouns; "Corporal" and "Airman" are the pilots' own words for themselves, and the king answers them by birth name. The king calls the steward, the petitioners and the pilots by their first names and the lieutenant "Dagmar Sund" and "Lieutenant". Aegon's letter says "Father and Mother", "Uncle Varys" and "Aunt Valia"; the king's letter is signed "Aeries" and begins "Varys", and the queen's is signed "Aalia" and begins "Valia". "Sire" is not spoken in this Chapter, and no wind-name or acknowledgment form is used.
  - (4) The sense rule as used. Korth, a Warrior, reads nothing across the water while the company holds its qi drawn in, then reads seventeen weights round the file once it lets go; her sense passes over the carters and the clerk as it passes over the mules; the raid leader's weight, far above her, reaches her knees before her eyes find it; Rask reads the captain by soul sense ("high in the seventh Level"); Aeries reads the plateau, the city and the people in it by the wind and finds no qi in the unawakened (the steward, the petitioners); a Senior draws her weight down before a Ruler; neither the king nor the queen shows more than the level.
  - (5) Time and place as used. The noon bell is named once (the king's "since the noon bell"); the Court rises when the light goes; the Stone Vigil lights its lamps at dusk and the keepers light the stations for the queen's hour; the wire stops at the corridor post and the rest is by glider; the *Kite Line* does not reach the mine road and stops in bad weather at the plateau's north edge; the Windward Trail opened to horses only days before the raid; Highgale lies an hour at a rider's pace to the east-south-east of the terrace; the Seam Road runs from Whistledeep down past Stirrup Ford (a ford with a gravel bar) and the Mule Stair to the plateau; Shalegate Pass is named once, as the pass above the ford, and is tied to no track.
  - (6) First-use facts of Gale that the page sets. The ward-line is a ring of stone marks and watch stations, each station a low stone hut with a dial under glass and a slate, and the keepers light their lamps for the queen's hour; the Court sits in the open on boards inside the Stone Ring and the king sits on a block of pale stone; a Gale letter is talk set down and the record is the people who heard it, read once aloud before named hearers; counts are written and promises are not; the Host copies its roll for the families; there is a long pause between the reading of a thing and its seal; the people of Gale lower their voices near the king, do not kneel, and say the blood is in a good mood tonight; birds leave a place he crosses and a dog will not lie down near him; the Court's rising is said by the steward and repeated by two sentries of the Stone Vigil.
  - (7) Points the author kept open or left as written at the write. The clause of D9 stays as written; Aalia stands beside the king "close" as written (D5); the payment of Aeries's costly behavior is deferred (D3); the stage of Hadric Stoll's Level 6 is unset (the page says "the sixth Level" and no stage); the nine Tier D names and the two sentries' levels are the drafting pass's picks (D11); "Brackenhold's king" is named once, in Rask's viewpoint, as stance only.
  - (8) What Aegon's letter shows of the school. Sixty wooden swords counted twice by Varya, the *First Grip* in blue put on upside down, Brea's sleeve torn off with her teeth, the rings of white sand, the best pupil who talks the whole time and the bout of five against one in which nobody won, and Kerra's "not yet". It names nothing of the ward, the road, the hurt of Phase Day 2 or his heritage.

**Chapter digest:**

Chapter 005, The House With No Walls (1.1.1.005). Novel-Part 1, The Two Mornings. Section 1, Azure Sky and Gale. Phase 1, The Roots of Becoming. Areas: 01 to 06 WRITTEN(1.1.1.005), built whole. (Novel-Part Spec 1.1.1 is not COMPLETED, so there is no Novel-Part line.)

Area 01, First Light at Stirrup Ford
- What changed: The reader has seen Gale's northern frontier and a Gale mine escort, six people met by what they do and placed by name and Level; the ambush has begun and the escort is split by the mist; the horn went out and nothing answered; a weight far above the captain has come out of the trees.
- Who: Sigrun Korth (viewpoint); Torvek Gant, Yrsa Wold and Ingrith Mott speak; Egil Hurn laughs and Jorund Ulm says nothing; the carters and the clerk are heard only as cries; the man in the good coat is a silent glimpse.
- Open threads: the weight (Area 02); the horn and the traces (Area 05); the standing orders (Areas 02, 05 and 06).

Area 02, The Count and the Sign
- What changed: The escort and the hands are dead and the company has the ore and the papers; the reader has seen the villain's own viewpoint a second time, with the sign and the count recognised; no one of the Gale caravan lives; how the company knew the road and the hour is not answered.
- Who: Wulfric Rask (viewpoint) gives one order; Hadric Stoll gives his squad one order; Sigrun Korth and the woman among the mules speak as they fall.
- Open threads: the question of the road and the hour (Areas 05 and 06); the book and papers taken; the traces the clearing missed (Area 05).

Area 03, The Wind Over Highgale
- What changed: The reader has seen Gale from above and from inside a king, and the man himself; his heritage is read in the people's way of looking, in the birds and the dog, and in no form; one technique is shown; his son's first letter home has reached him with a question.
- Who: Aeries (viewpoint); Ranulf Skeld, Ottar Haug, Hallgerd Vorn and Ivar Lund speak; Knut Dahl is named; Aalia is sensed and not seen.
- Open threads: the boy's question (Area 04, and left open); the drain case (Area 05).

Area 04, The Ward-Walk at Dusk
- What changed: The reader has met the queen and seen her walk her ward and set it right; one technique is shown; her son's absence is placed in the house; the king and the queen have shared the letter and spoken of the friends in Azorion; nothing of the raid has reached them.
- Who: Aalia (viewpoint); Ragna Holm and Aeries speak.
- Open threads: Valia's note (Area 06); the boy's question, answered with a place and left open; "the gate" as a place and no plan.

Area 05, What the Pilot Carried
- What changed: The Gale court has heard of two raids and holds one question and one clue it cannot test; the king has been told by his friend, in his friend's hand, what his friend held back and why; the lieutenant's count, read from the ground, has made the second raid real for the Court; the Captain's company is without its standing orders; nothing has been promised.
- Who: Aeries (viewpoint); Sverre Vik and Dagmar Sund speak, she at length; Knut Dahl's argument is reported; Aalia, Ottar Haug, Ranulf Skeld, Gudrun Nyland and Leif Bakke are on the page and say nothing; Varys speaks in his letter.
- Open threads: the king's question (Area 06); the standing orders; the clamp.

Area 06, Two Seals
- What changed: The two courts hold both raids, with no source named; the king has told his friend plainly and bound nothing; the queen has told hers everything; the Chapter's last image is two seals and a question under the wax of each.
- Who: Aalia (viewpoint); Aeries speaks and reads his letter aloud; Dagmar Sund answers the one question; Ranulf Skeld, Gudrun Nyland and Leif Bakke say the Court's rising; Sverre Vik is on the page; Varys and Valia are present by their letters.
- Open threads: the question of the road and the day (a later Novel-Part); Valia's third line, to be answered "at the gate"; the standing orders.

Accepted variances (D1 to D13; the full notes are in the Audit and Settlement Notes). Author-ruled or kept on purpose: D3 (the deferral of the payment of Aeries's costly behavior), D5 ("beside him, close") and D9 (the clause "until the king had spoken it, standing, before named witnesses"). Recorded from the Maker's hand-off and this lane's reading of the text, and carried by the author's `[CONFIRM WRITE]` of the Chapter as it stands: D1, D2, D8, D10 and D11 (the Gate 1 list that shows which of them were listed and which were core differences traced to an answer is in the session record). Information recorded by this lane with no ruling asked: D4, D6, D7, D12 and D13.

**New named things met by the reader this Chapter** (the header line, completed from the written Chapter): see the Chapter Header Block, section 1.

## 7 — Audit and Settlement Notes

This section is the audit trail of the spec (Part 38 Rule 9.10.2). It is a plain list, kept in the order the notes were made within each group, each note beginning with the plan item it concerns. The body above is plain plan text. Dates are ISO. Every note about the author's answers carries 2026-10-18, the day the author's rulings for this Chapter were relayed by the orchestrating command and the day this draft was assembled; an Inherited note carries the date of the answer or deferral it brings down and the date it was carried here. The groups run: Whole spec and header; From the Novel-Part Spec entry; Inherited from Novel-Part Spec 1.1.1; Inherited from Section Spec 1.1; Deferred; Settled; Skipped; Names; Open; Variance; Audit; and, last, the group "As-built sync at the Gate 2 of /create-chapter". This was a first build when the notes of the build were made, with nothing written and every Area block PLANNED (corrected, a correction of record, at the Gate 2 of 2026-10-19: the Chapter is written and every Area block reads WRITTEN(1.1.1.005); the notes of the build stay as the history of the plan, and the last group brings them up to date). The spec was assembled as a DRAFT and the author confirmed it on 2026-10-18 (see the confirm note in the first group). A note that carries the words "planner chose" settles nothing, and the final sweep re-lists each one for the author's last chance.

The Continuity Checker's light audit, the final sweep, the author's word that nothing is left to steer and [CONFIRM SPEC] have not yet happened. The headers of both parents record that their own light audits and cascade reports are owed to the next Continuity Checker pass (2026-10-18).
- **Header, Spec Status, the revision of 2026-10-18** — Audit: the first draft was handed back on 2026-10-18 and revised the same day for six rulings the author gave in chat afterward and the orchestrating command relayed: Dagmar Sund is a Lieutenant; no one of the Gale caravan lives; the Captain's death costs her company its standing orders, in one plain line left open; Aeries's technique stays at dusk on the open edge; Varys's letter carries the wire clamp as one plain confirmed fact; the levels as drafted are confirmed. Every dependent field and note is updated; the planner's picks these rulings contradict (the rank Sergeant, the hurt carter found alive, the omission of the clamp, and the late-night hour as a flagged departure) are removed, and the three Open notes they close (the standing orders, Aeries's practice time and the plan values) are replaced by Settled notes.
